Here's the plan. Note: I checked and the "Quality-analysis workflow" already exists at `.claude/skills/bmad-workflow-builder/references/quality-analysis.md` with its scan scripts — no need to self-improve it.

## The Core elevation took 17 PRs. Here's how we do 5 skills in 2.

**Why Core needed 17:** Most of those PRs were *new* shared-script extractions (#308–#316) that each needed test wiring. That backbone is now built. The Feature skills get to ride it instead of rebuilding it.

## Genius approach: parallel-scan → batch-merge

**Phase 1 — One scan pass, run in parallel (single message, 5 subagents)**
Spawn 5 `Explore` agents simultaneously, each running the Quality-analysis workflow against one Feature skill (QS, SS, AN, AS, TS). Each returns a finding-coded report (Q1, S1, A1…). Cost is bound by the slowest scan, not by N.

**Phase 2 — Synthesize cross-skill patterns (single pass)**
Merge the 5 reports into one ranked table. Group by *type* (customize.toml parity, language headers, headless contract, UX polish, shared-script opportunity) rather than by *skill*. This is where the genius compresses 5×N findings into ~2N action items.

**Phase 3 — ONE Foundation PR (the #306 analog)**
Mechanical parity across all 5 skills in a single PR:
- `customize.toml` with three append-arrays + any path-scalar candidates
- Language-config headers on every stage file
- Cancel-line / UX polish (B6 pattern)
- Cross-skill coupling docs where applicable

**Phase 4 — ONE Tightening PR (the #323 analog)**
One PR sweeping every low-severity actionable finding across all 5 skills. Defer substantive items (new shared extractions, new headless modes) to a tracking issue.

**Total: 2 PRs.** Optional 3rd PR only if scans surface a *genuinely new* shared-extractable script or a headless-gap that's high-value enough to justify carving.

## Tradeoff you should know about

Big multi-skill PRs are harder to review than small focused ones. We trade reviewer ergonomics for ship velocity. Mitigation: the PR body's findings table (coded items like in #306/#323) makes the diff self-navigable.

1	# Quality Analysis
2	
3	Communicate with user in `{communication_language}`. Write report content in `{document_output_language}`.
4	
5	You orchestrate quality analysis on a BMad workflow or skill. The pipeline is optimized for speed and completeness:
6	
7	1. **Deterministic checks** (scripts) — zero tokens, instant
8	2. **LLM scanners** (parallel subagents) — judgment-based analysis against `skill-quality-principles.md`
9	3. **Fast JSON extraction** (deterministic script) — lossless capture of all scanner findings (~10 seconds, no LLM)
10	4. **HTML generation** — interactive, auto-opening report from JSON (no wait for synthesis)
11	5. **Optional markdown synthesis** (LLM subagent, background) — thematic analysis and archival markdown
12	
13	The scanners verify against `references/skill-quality-principles.md` — the same file the build process loads at create/edit time. Findings cite the principle that's being violated rather than restating it.
14	
15	## Your Role: Coordination, Not File Reading
16	
17	**Do not read the target skill's files yourself.** Scripts and subagents do all analysis. You orchestrate: run deterministic scripts and pre-pass extractors, spawn LLM scanner subagents in parallel, hand off to the report creator for synthesis.
18	
19	## Headless Mode
20	
21	If `{headless_mode}=true`, skip user interaction, use safe defaults, note any warnings, and output structured JSON as specified in the Present Findings section.
22	
23	## Pre-Scan Checks
24	
25	Check for uncommitted changes. In headless mode, note warnings and proceed. In interactive mode, inform the user, confirm before proceeding, and confirm the workflow is currently functioning.
26	
27	## Analysis Principles
28	
29	**Effectiveness over efficiency.** The analysis may suggest leaner phrasing, but if the current phrasing captures the right guidance, it should be kept. The report presents opportunities — the user applies judgment.
30	
31	## Scanners
32	
33	### Lint Scripts (Deterministic — Run First)
34	
35	Run instantly, cost zero tokens, produce structured JSON:
36	
37	| #  | Script                           | Focus                                   | Output File                |
38	| -- | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------- |
39	| S1 | `scripts/scan-path-standards.py` | Path conventions                        | `path-standards-temp.json` |
40	| S2 | `scripts/scan-scripts.py`        | Script portability, PEP 723, unit tests | `scripts-temp.json`        |
41	
42	### Pre-Pass Scripts (Feed LLM Scanners)
43	
44	Extract metrics so LLM scanners work from compact data instead of raw files:
45	
46	| #  | Script                                  | Feeds                  | Output File                       |
47	| -- | --------------------------------------- | ---------------------- | --------------------------------- |
48	| P1 | `scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py` | architecture scanner   | `workflow-integrity-prepass.json` |
49	| P2 | `scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py`     | architecture scanner   | `prompt-metrics-prepass.json`     |
50	| P3 | `scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py`     | determinism scanner    | `execution-deps-prepass.json`     |
51	
52	### LLM Scanners (Judgment-Based — Run After Scripts)
53	
54	Each scanner loads `references/skill-quality-principles.md` and writes a free-form analysis document:
55	
56	| #  | Scanner                              | Focus                                                                          | Pre-Pass | Output File                  |
57	| -- | ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------- | ---------------------------- |
58	| L1 | `quality-scan-architecture.md`       | Structural integrity, prose craft, cohesion (was: integrity + craft + cohesion)| Yes (P1, P2) | `architecture-analysis.md`   |
59	| L2 | `quality-scan-determinism.md`        | Intelligence placement, parallelization, subagent delegation, script opportunities (was: execution-efficiency + script-opportunities) | Yes (P3) | `determinism-analysis.md`    |
60	| L3 | `quality-scan-customization.md`      | customize.toml opportunities and abuse                                         | No       | `customization-analysis.md`  |
61	| L4 | `quality-scan-enhancement.md`        | Edge cases, UX gaps, headless potential, facilitative patterns                 | No       | `enhancement-analysis.md`    |
62	
63	## Execution
64	
65	Bind `{quality-report-dir} = {skill-path}/.analysis/{date-time-stamp}/` and create the directory. Use this single name in every script invocation and subagent prompt below. Quality analyses live at the skill's own root, as a peer of `.decision-log.md` and `SKILL.md` — the audit trail travels with the skill.
66	
67	### Step 1: Run All Scripts (Parallel)
68	
69	```bash
70	python3 scripts/scan-path-standards.py {skill-path} -o {quality-report-dir}/path-standards-temp.json
71	python3 scripts/scan-scripts.py {skill-path} -o {quality-report-dir}/scripts-temp.json
72	uv run scripts/prepass-workflow-integrity.py {skill-path} -o {quality-report-dir}/workflow-integrity-prepass.json
73	python3 scripts/prepass-prompt-metrics.py {skill-path} -o {quality-report-dir}/prompt-metrics-prepass.json
74	uv run scripts/prepass-execution-deps.py {skill-path} -o {quality-report-dir}/execution-deps-prepass.json
75	```
76	
77	### Step 2: Spawn LLM Scanners (Parallel)
78	
79	After scripts complete, spawn all four LLM scanners as parallel subagents.
80	
81	Each subagent receives:
82	- Scanner file to load
83	- Skill path: `{skill-path}`
84	- Output directory: `{quality-report-dir}`
85	- Pre-pass file paths (L1: P1+P2; L2: P3)
86	
87	The subagent loads its scanner file (which loads the principles file), analyzes the skill, writes its analysis to `{quality-report-dir}`, and returns the filename.
88	
89	### Step 3: Synthesize Report (Parallel with Scanner 4)
90	
91	Spawn report creator to synthesize scanner outputs into `report-data.json` and `quality-report.md`. This can run in parallel with the last scanner finishing.
92	
93	```bash
94	# Spawn as background task — does not block step 4
95	Agent(description="Synthesize quality report", subagent_type="report-creator", run_in_background=true, prompt="...")
96	```
97	
98	The report creator:
99	- Reads all 4 analysis files + prepass JSON
100	- Identifies thematic clusters (root-cause synthesis)
101	- Writes `report-data.json` with: broken, opportunities, strengths, recommendations, detailed_analysis
102	- Writes `quality-report.md` for archival
103	
104	### Step 4: Generate & Open HTML Report (Do Not Block on Markdown)
105	
106	As soon as `report-data.json` exists (the report creator writes it mid-synthesis), generate the interactive HTML report:
107	
108	```bash
109	python3 scripts/generate-html-report.py {quality-report-dir} --open
110	```
111	
112	**Important:** Do not wait for `quality-report.md` to be written. The JSON is the complete data source. Open HTML immediately. The markdown report finishes asynchronously and provides archival context.
113	
114	### Step 5: Log the Run
115	
116	After HTML opens, append a session heading to `{skill-path}/.decision-log.md`:
117	
118	```markdown
119	## YYYY-MM-DD — Quality analysis
120	
121	Grade: <grade from report-data.json>. Interactive HTML: `.analysis/<timestamp>/quality-report.html`. Full markdown: `.analysis/<timestamp>/quality-report.md`.
122	```
123	
124	## Present to User
125	
126	**Headless** (`{headless_mode}=true`): emit JSON only.
127	
128	```json
129	{
130	  "status": "complete",
131	  "intent": "analyze",
132	  "skill": "{skill-path}",
133	  "decision_log": "{skill-path}/.decision-log.md",
134	  "report": "{quality-report-dir}/quality-report.md"
135	}
136	```
137	
138	Blocked (scanner failure, missing required input, etc.): replace `"complete"` with `"blocked"` and add `"reason": "<one-line cause>"`. The log + any partial report carry the detail.
139	
140	**Interactive:** read `report-data.json` and present grade + 2-3 sentence narrative, broken items if any, top opportunities by theme, paths to the full report and HTML. Offer to apply fixes, walk findings, or discuss.
141	
```

1	# BMad Quality Analysis Report Creator
2	
3	You synthesize scanner output into a unified, actionable quality report. Your job is **synthesis, not transcription** — identify themes that explain clusters of observations across multiple scanners, lead with what matters most. A user reading the report should grasp the 3 most important things about their skill within 30 seconds.
4	
5	## Inputs
6	
7	- `{skill-path}` — the skill being analyzed
8	- `{quality-report-dir}` — directory with all scanner output and where you write the report
9	
10	## Read
11	
12	- `*-temp.json` — lint script output (structured findings)
13	- `*-prepass.json` — pre-pass metrics
14	- `*-analysis.md` — LLM scanner analyses (free-form): `architecture-analysis.md`, `determinism-analysis.md`, `customization-analysis.md`, `enhancement-analysis.md`
15	
16	## Synthesize Themes
17	
18	This is the most important step. Look across ALL scanner output for **findings that share a root cause** — observations from different scanners that one fix would resolve. Ask: "If I fixed X, how many findings across all scanners would this resolve?"
19	
20	Group related findings into 3-5 themes. Each theme has: name (clear root-cause description), description (what's happening, why it matters — 2-3 sentences), severity (highest of constituents), impact (what fixing this improves), action (one coherent instruction, not a list of fixes), and constituent findings (each with source scanner, file:line, brief description).
21	
22	Findings that don't fit any theme become standalone items.
23	
24	## Assess Overall Quality
25	
26	- **Grade:** Excellent (no high+ issues, few medium) / Good (some high or several medium) / Fair (multiple high) / Poor (critical issues)
27	- **Narrative:** 2-3 sentences capturing the skill's primary strength and primary opportunity. This is what the user reads first.
28	
29	## Write Two Files
30	
31	### 1. quality-report.md
32	
33	```markdown
34	# BMad Quality Analysis: {skill-name}
35	
36	**Analyzed:** {timestamp} | **Path:** {skill-path}
37	**Interactive report:** quality-report.html
38	
39	## Assessment
40	
41	**{Grade}** — {narrative}
42	
43	## What's Broken
44	
45	{Only if critical/high issues exist. Each with file:line, what's wrong, how to fix.}
46	
47	## Opportunities
48	
49	### 1. {Theme Name} ({severity} — {N} observations)
50	
51	{Description.} **Fix:** {One coherent action.}
52	
53	**Observations:**
54	- {finding} — file:line
55	- ...
56	
57	{Repeat for each theme.}
58	
59	## Strengths
60	
61	{What works — preserve these.}
62	
63	## Detailed Analysis
64	
65	### Architecture
66	{Assessment + findings not covered by themes (structural integrity, prose craft, cohesion).}
67	
68	### Determinism & Distribution
69	{Assessment + findings (intelligence placement, parallelization, script opportunities).}
70	
71	### Customization Surface
72	{Assessment + opportunities and abuse findings.}
73	
74	### User Experience
75	{Journeys, headless assessment, facilitative-pattern check, edge cases.}
76	
77	## Recommendations
78	
79	1. {Highest impact — resolves N observations}
80	2. ...
81	```
82	
83	### 2. report-data.json
84

The four `detailed_analysis` keys are `architecture`, `determinism`, `customization`, `enhancement`.

1	# Skill Quality Principles
2	
3	What earns its place in a BMad skill, and what should be cut. Loaded at both build time (so the author follows the bar upfront) and at quality-analysis time (so scanners verify against the same bar).
4	
5	## The Core Test
6	
7	For every line you write or review: **would an LLM do this correctly without being told?** If yes, cut it. The instruction must earn its place by preventing a failure that would otherwise happen.
8	
9	## What Earns Its Keep
10	
11	The model already knows how to facilitate, ask questions, write prose, parse intent, and format markdown. Spend file weight on:
12	
13	- **Project paths and outputs** — `{project-root}/...`, config-resolved paths, where the artifact lands.
14	- **Schema** — frontmatter format, customize.toml shape, downstream contracts.
15	- **BMad-specific conventions** — naming (`bmad-` prefix, module prefixes), description format, intelligence placement.
16	- **Hard rules with body count** — the implicit-read trap, subagent-can't-spawn-subagent, compaction survival.
17	- **Fragile-operation invocations** — exact script commands, exact API calls. One right way.
18	- **Domain framing and theory-of-mind** for interactive workflows — context that enables judgment.
19	- **Design rationale** for non-obvious choices — prevents the LLM from "optimizing" away constraints it doesn't understand.
20	
21	## What Doesn't Earn Its Keep
22	
23	- Numbered procedural steps for things the LLM does naturally
24	- Per-platform adapter files for tools the LLM speaks fluently
25	- Scoring formulas, weighted calibration tables, decision matrices for subjective judgment
26	- Templates teaching output formatting, greeting users, or prompt assembly
27	- "Why It Matters" prose attached to obvious checks
28	- Defensive padding ("make sure", "don't forget", "remember to")
29	- Meta-explanation ("This workflow is designed to...")
30	- Bot personas with rubrics where role + outcome would do the same job
31	- Explaining the model to itself ("You are an AI that...")
32	- Multiple files that could be a single instruction
33	
34	## Outcome vs Prescriptive
35	
36	| Prescriptive (avoid) | Outcome-based (prefer) |
37	| --- | --- |
38	| "Step 1: Ask about goals. Step 2: Ask about constraints. Step 3: Summarize and confirm." | "Ensure the user's vision is fully captured — goals, constraints, and edge cases — before proceeding." |
39	| "Load config. Read user_name. Read communication_language. Greet by name in their language." | "Load available config and greet the user appropriately." |
40	| "Create a file. Write the header. Write section 1. Write section 2. Save." | "Produce a report covering X, Y, and Z." |
41	
42	The prescriptive versions miss requirements the author didn't think of. The outcome-based versions let the LLM adapt.
43	
44	## When Procedure IS Value
45	
46	Reserve exact steps for fragile operations where deviation has consequences:
47	
48	- Exact script invocations (`python3 scripts/foo.py {arg}`)
49	- Specific file paths and config keys
50	- API calls with precise parameters
51	- Security-critical operations
52	- The customize.toml resolver step
53	
54	| Freedom | When | Example |
55	| --- | --- | --- |
56	| **High** (outcomes) | Multiple valid approaches, LLM judgment adds value | "Ensure the user's requirements are complete" |
57	| **Medium** (guided) | Preferred approach exists, some variation OK | "Present findings in a structured report with an executive summary" |
58	| **Low** (exact) | Fragile, one right way, consequences for deviation | `python3 scripts/scan-path-standards.py {skill-path}` |
59	
60	## BMad Institutional Knowledge
61	
62	Things the bare model genuinely won't know. This is what your file weight buys.
63	
64	### Naming
65	- Skill name = folder name (kebab-case)
66	- Module skill: `{module-code}-{name}` (e.g. `bmm-create-prd`, `cis-brainstorm`)
67	- Standalone: `{name}`
68	- The `bmad-` prefix is reserved for official BMad creations
69	
70	### Description format
71	Two parts: `[5-8 word summary]. [Use when user says 'specific phrase' or 'specific phrase'.]`
72	
73	Quote the trigger phrases. Default to conservative (explicit) triggering — most BMad skills are explicitly invoked. Organic triggering is reserved for skills that should activate on context (e.g. "Trigger when code imports anthropic SDK").
74	
75	Bad: `Helps with PRDs and product requirements.` (too vague — hijacks unrelated conversations).
76	
77	### Path conventions
78	All file references in a skill use bare paths from the skill root. The canonical Conventions block (from `bmad-prfaq/SKILL.md`) — stamp it into any SKILL.md that references multiple internal files:
79	
80	```
81	## Conventions
82	- Bare paths (e.g. `references/press-release.md`) resolve from the skill root.
83	- `{skill-root}` resolves to this skill's installed directory (where `customize.toml` lives).
84	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
85	- `{skill-name}` resolves to the skill directory's basename.
86	```
87	
88	Additional rules:
89	- Forward slashes only (cross-platform).
90	- Config variables already contain `{project-root}` in their resolved values — never double-prefix.
91	- `references/` is for prompt content carved out of SKILL.md. `assets/` is for templates and other static content the workflow loads. `scripts/` is for deterministic code. Never put workflow content directly at skill root.
92	
93	### Customization (customize.toml)
94	Always-present fields: `activation_steps_prepend`, `activation_steps_append`, `persistent_facts` (each is an array; overrides append).
95	
96	Workflow-specific scalars (lifted during configurability discovery):
97	- `<purpose>_template` for template file paths
98	- `<purpose>_output_path` for writable destinations
99	- `on_<event>` for hook scalars
100	
101	Arrays of tables MUST key on `code` or `id` (resolver merges by key; without it, falls back to append-only).
102	
103	Merge rules: scalars override, tables deep-merge, arrays-of-tables key-merge, plain arrays append.
104	
105	Override files: `{project-root}/_bmad/custom/{skill-name}.toml` (team), `.user.toml` (personal). Merge order: base → team → user.
106	
107	Default `persistent_facts`: `["file:{project-root}/**/project-context.md"]` is BMad's convention.
108	
109	SKILL.md must reference resolved values as `{workflow.<name>}`. Hardcoded paths next to a declared scalar = override silently no-ops.
110	
111	### Intelligence placement
112	- Scripts handle plumbing: fetch, parse, validate, count, transform.
113	- Prompts handle judgment: interpret, classify, decide.
114	- Script using regex to decide what content MEANS = intelligence leak into the script.
115	- Prompt validating structure, counting items, comparing against schemas = determinism leak into the LLM.
116	
117	### Workflows: inline first, carve out only when needed
118	Default: write the entire workflow as named sections in SKILL.md (`## Discovery`, `## Constraints`, `## Finalize`, etc.). A multi-stage coaching workflow can live in one SKILL.md.
119	
120	Carve out to `references/` only when SKILL.md genuinely gets too big to scan. When you do:
121	- **Descriptive filenames.** `references/press-release.md`, `references/customer-faq.md`. Never numbered prefixes (`01-press-release.md`) — the carve-out is a section, not a "step." SKILL.md routes to references by name and the order is whatever SKILL.md specifies.
122	- Each carved-out file works standalone — context compaction can drop SKILL.md mid-flow. No "as described in the overview."
123	- Progression conditions, where they exist, must be testable ("when X is captured, route to Y"). "When ready" is vague.
124	- The file uses `{communication_language}` (and `{document_output_language}` if it produces a doc).
125	- There are NO exit hooks in the system. Don't add `## On Exit` sections — they'd never run.
126	
127	### Headless mode
128	
129	When a skill supports headless invocation, the decision log absorbs every assumption made without the user — intent inference, proposed names, customization defaults, conflict resolutions, lint-fix calls, anything the user would have weighed in on interactively. The JSON return is the smallest set of paths the caller needs (typically `skill` + `decision_log`, plus the report path for analysis flows); the log carries the reasoning. `status` is `complete` or `blocked`; on `blocked`, include a one-line `reason` and still return the log path so the caller can read the detail. Without this discipline, headless silently buries its calls and the audit trail breaks on the next session.
130	
131	### Subagent constraints
132	- Subagents CANNOT spawn other subagents. Chain through parent.
133	- Don't read files in parent if you can delegate the read — parent stays lean.
134	- Subagent prompts must specify exact return format and "ONLY return X" constraint, or you get verbose prose.
135	- **The implicit-read trap:** Language like "review", "acknowledge", "summarize what you have" causes the parent to read files even when you didn't ask for it. If a later stage delegates document analysis, earlier stages must NOT use that language. Use "note paths for subagent scanning; don't read them now".
136	
137	### Size guidance
138	Production targets, not hard limits. The "what fails if I delete this?" test still applies to every line.
139	
140	- SKILL.md: ~80 lines target, hard ceiling ~130
141	- Multi-branch SKILL.md: up to ~250 lines if each branch has brief contextual explanation
142	- Single-purpose: up to ~500 lines (~5000 tokens) if focused
143	- Past those: lift to `references/` or `assets/`
144	
145	### Patterns BMad has seen pay off
146	Institutional names for patterns the LLM won't generate by default:
147	
148	- **Open-floor opening** — Conversational skills start with an explicit invitation for the user to share everything they have (goals, references, examples, paths to artifacts) before any structured Q&A. The dump replaces most of the question script that would otherwise follow; the agent then asks only what's missing. The form adapts to input — vague request gets "tell me everything", path/URL gets "what do you want focused on?". Costs almost nothing token-wise; drastically improves conversational feel.
149	- **Soft-gate elicitation** — "Anything else, or shall we move on?" at natural transitions. Users always remember one more thing when given a graceful exit.
150	- **Intent-before-ingestion** — Understand why the user is here before scanning artifacts. Without intent, scanning is noise.
151	- **Capture-don't-interrupt** — Out-of-scope insights mid-flow get captured silently, not redirected. Users in flow share their best stuff unprompted.
152	- **Dual-output** — Human artifact + LLM distillate, when the artifact will feed downstream agents.
153	- **Parallel review lenses** — Fan out 2-3 review subagents (skeptic, opportunity-spotter, contextually-chosen lens) before finalizing significant artifacts.
154	- **Three-mode architecture** — Guided / Yolo / Headless. Not all skills need all three; considering it during design prevents lock-in.
155	- **Graceful degradation** — Subagent-dependent features fall back to sequential when subagents are unavailable.
156	- **Decision-Log Workspace** — multi-turn workflows producing revisable artifacts. The decision log is the load-bearing artifact (carries identity across sessions, prevents railroading, audits overrides). Subsumes "document-as-cache" — see full treatment below.
157	
158	### Writing
159	- One term per concept; pick it and stick to it.
160	- Third person in descriptions ("Processes files", not "I help process files").
161	- Descriptive file names (`form-validation-rules.md`, not `doc2.md`).
162	- One level deep for reference files — SKILL.md → reference, never SKILL → ref → ref chains.
163	
164	## The Decision-Log Workspace Pattern
165	
166	The default for any multi-turn workflow that produces a substantive artifact, may be revisited (Update or Validate), or risks running long enough to compact.
167	
168	**Core insight.** The decision log is the load-bearing artifact, not the document. The document is what the user takes; the decision log is what carries identity across sessions, prevents the agent from railroading the user, surfaces conflicts on update, and creates an audit trail when the user overrides their own past calls. Workflows that lack it look fine on the first pass and fall apart on revisit.
169	
170	### Workspace layout
171	
172	All files live in a single folder rooted at the primary artifact. Two cases:
173	
174	- **The artifact is a single document** (a brief, a PRFAQ, etc.) → the workspace is the document's containing folder; the log + addendum + distillate sit as peers of the document.
175	- **The artifact is itself a folder of files** (a built skill, a generated module) → the workspace IS the artifact's folder; the log + addendum sit as peers of the primary file (e.g. `SKILL.md`).
176	
177	Either way, the workspace exists from the moment intent is confirmed — not at the end. The user knows the path immediately; state lives on disk, not in the conversation.
178	
179	- `<primary>` — the artifact (or, for folder-artifacts, the primary file like `SKILL.md`). YAML frontmatter is the recoverable-state mechanism when the workflow needs it; fields are workflow-specific (the LLM picks what each workflow benefits from — some need none).
180	- `.decision-log.md` — every meaningful decision and why, with alternatives considered. Append-only across sessions, with date-stamped session headings. Can carry its own frontmatter for session state when that's useful.
181	- `addendum.md` — context the user surfaced that didn't earn a place in the primary (rejected alternatives, parked roadmap, options-considered matrices, in-depth personas). Created only when something earns its place.
182	- `distillate.md` *(optional)* — token-efficient version of the primary for downstream LLM consumers.
183	
184	### Resume protocol
185	
186	On activation, check whether a workspace already exists for this artifact. If found, surface it (with the `updated` timestamp from the primary's frontmatter) and offer to resume. Reading `.decision-log.md` recovers full context regardless of compaction.
187	
188	### Update mode
189	
190	Read `.decision-log.md` and the addendum first. The change request enters as a "change signal" against the standing record. If the change contradicts a prior decision, surface the conflict before applying. Every change — clean or override — gets a new decision-log entry. Overrides also write to the addendum: the rejected reasoning needs to live somewhere.
191	
192	### Validate mode
193	
194	Read `.decision-log.md` first. A validation that ignores prior decisions or stated user criteria is shallow; it should challenge the artifact against the standards the user themselves set, not against generic rubrics.
195	
196	### Finalize step
197	
198	Decision-log audit. Every meaningful entry must be either captured in the primary, captured in the addendum, or explicitly set aside as process noise. The user ends the session with a shared accounting of how their thinking was handled — not a one-sided polish-and-deliver.
199	
200	### When NOT to use
201	
202	- Simple Utilities (no decisions to log; the input/output IS the contract).
203	- One-shot code operations (the diff is the decision log).
204	- Purely conversational skills (no artifact persists).
205	
206	### Treatment style (writing it into a skill)
207	
208	State the principle once where it first applies — typically inside the Create intent description as a single clause ("write the primary skeleton and `.decision-log.md` to the workspace; the decision log is canonical memory"). Mention reads at the moments that matter: Update reads decisions before changing them, Validate reads them before critiquing, Finalize audits the log at handoff. That's the entire treatment.
209	
210	Do NOT:
211	- Open with a "Decision-log discipline" enumeration of what kinds of things to log — the LLM knows. Trust it.
212	- Write a separate `## Workspace` section header with meta-explanation of the pattern.
213	- Include a tree diagram of the workspace layout — the workspace is just files; the LLM names them as it uses them.
214	- Prescribe a YAML frontmatter schema for the decision log — fields are workflow-specific; let the building LLM pick what each workflow needs (or skip frontmatter entirely).
215	- Split workspace creation into separate "for new" / "for existing" sub-sections — "create if absent, append a new session heading if present" is one sentence.
216	
217	The scanner flags skills that bury DLW guidance under ceremony. `bmad-product-brief` is the canonical-brief example: ~5 sentences total, threaded through Create / Update / Validate / Constraints / Finalize at the points where each matters.
218	
219	## Failure Modes With Body Count
220	
221	- **Description over-broadens** → Skill hijacks unrelated conversations. Fix: quote trigger phrases.
222	- **Vague progression conditions** ("when ready") → Stage never advances or advances early. Fix: testable conditions.
223	- **Stage references SKILL.md** ("as above") → Breaks on compaction. Fix: stages self-contained.
224	- **Subagent prompt without explicit return format** → Verbose prose responses. Fix: "Return ONLY {schema}. No other output."
225	- **Parent reads then delegates analysis** → Context bloat that makes delegation pointless. Fix: delegate the read.
226	- **Implicit-read trap** in a stage that precedes subagent delegation → Parent reads everything anyway. Fix: explicit "don't read these now".
227	- **Scoring formulas for subjective judgment** → Rigidity that doesn't improve quality. Fix: state the outcome, let the model assess.
228	- **Boolean toggles in customize.toml** → Author didn't decide what the skill does; surface becomes a permutation forest. Fix: pick a default; users fork if they want the other shape.
229	- **Hardcoded path in SKILL.md while customize.toml declares the scalar** → Override silently does nothing. Fix: SKILL.md must read `{workflow.<name>}`.
230	- **Identity / communication-style / principles in `[workflow]`** → Workflow wants to be an agent. Fix: point author at agent-builder; remove from workflow surface.
231	
```

Single Python script, 1 file, 0 lint issues.\n- **workflow-integrity:** 0 issues, all 7 H2 sections present, valid frontmatter, simple-workflow classification matches inline-stages-table reality. (Note: stage_summary.total_stages reads `0` — the prepass keys on `## Stages` direct-child stages, not `references/`-folder enumeration; behavior is correct.)\n- **prompt-metrics:** SKILL.md 67 lines / ~865 tokens — well under the ~130-line ceiling. References vary: `report.md` 290 lines / 3995 tokens and `coverage-check.md` 347 lines / 5323 tokens are the heaviest; both in single-purpose ≤5000 token range but lean against it. Zero waste patterns detected, zero back-references — strong.\n- **execution-deps:** 0 issues — no inferable hard/soft dependency graph because chaining is encoded through frontmatter `nextStepFile`, which the prepass doesn't parse. Chain is linear (init → detect-mode → coverage → coherence → external → score → report → health-check) and correctly serializable.\n\n## Strengths (preserve)\n\n- Three-state PASS/FAIL/INCONCLUSIVE gate with a centralized minimum-evidence floor (scoring-rules.md:175-188) — exactly the failsafe principles-file calls out for a grader skill.\n- The deterministic scoring script (scripts/compute-score.py) implements the floor centrally, with a clear \"Never override the script's `result` value\" instruction at score.md:110.\n- M3 equivalence-class matrix at scoring-rules.md:103-150 — redistribution math captured as data, not prose, with 26 cells reducing to 4 classes. Pinning fixtures by class is the right testing strategy.\n- Subagent inventory contract at coverage-check.md:78-94 — parent-side schema validation + ground-truth spot-check is exactly the \"ONLY return X + verify\" discipline principles-file demands. (Apply consistently — see D6.)\n- Source-Access-Protocol five-state waterfall (source-access-protocol.md:74-103) — handles the local/provenance-map/metadata/remote/docs-only continuum without confidence inflation; `analysis_confidence` propagated downstream.\n- Run-id + flock (init.md:184-186) — per-run identifier + per-skill test lock makes the workflow safe under CI concurrency.\n- `pass-with-drift` (score.md:158-170, report.md:158, 228-229, 247) — the test result records that the verdict cannot be trusted for export, and next-workflow is forced to `update-skill`. The kind of design-rationale-as-mechanism principles-file rewards.\n\n## Recommendations (top 3 ranked by impact)\n\n1. **Ship customize.toml + reference-file language headers (A1, A2, C1, C2)** — effort: low. Unblocks org adoption (forks today, configurable tomorrow), brings the skill in line with the Excellent peer's shape, adds the persistent_facts surface where org-specific severity policies will naturally live. Resolves 4 findings.\n\n2. **Carve `scripts/scan-skill-md-structure.py` + add compute-score.py tests (D1, D2, D3, D4, D5)** — effort: medium. Replaces ~5 LLM-orchestrated grep/awk recipes per coverage+coherence run with a single script call (token savings: several thousand per invocation at large skills) and pins the scoring math against the M3 fixture matrix. Resolves 5 findings.\n\n3. **Tighten reference-file boilerplate + lift contract to SKILL.md (A4, A5, A6, A7, E1, E2)** — effort: medium. Cuts ~150 lines of repeated step-file ceremony, drops the redundant `health-check.md` passthrough, surfaces exit codes + `--allow-workspace-drift` at the SKILL.md contract level where automators look. Resolves 6 findings.\n"

1	# Quality Findings: skf-quick-skill
2	
3	**Grade:** Good
4	**Narrative:** This skill is structurally well-architected and intelligence-placement-aware — sophisticated probe-order helper resolution, ref-aware source reading, explicit parallel-fetch directives, a stable Exit Codes map with paired HARD-HALT envelope contracts, a Batch Mode with per-target boundary events, and a Repo-Shape Sniff that gracefully degrades non-library targets. The primary gap to Excellent is one runtime path bug (`{forger_root}` does not resolve — `preferences.yaml` will never be read), a missing `customize.toml` despite at least three lift-worthy paths (template, registries data, batch output), and an exit-code/envelope hole on the ecosystem-check [I] Install / [A] Abort branches.
5	
6	## Broken (critical/high)
7	
8	- **{forger_root} variable does not resolve** | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:116
9	  Issue: `{forger_root}` is not defined anywhere in the module (`module.yaml` declares `sidecar_path`, not `forger_root`; no other skill references `forger_root`). The line `Read \`{project-root}/_bmad/skf/config.yaml\` and \`{forger_root}/preferences.yaml\` in parallel` therefore reads a literal `{forger_root}/preferences.yaml` path that doesn't exist, so `headless_mode` resolution falls through to the default `false`. Headless invocations via `preferences.yaml` will silently fail; only `--headless`/`-H` arg-based activation works.
10	  Fix: Replace `{forger_root}` with `{sidecar_path}` (matches `skf-create-skill/references/load-brief.md:4` and `skf-forger/SKILL.md:54` pattern). Also extend On-Activation §1 config read to include `sidecar_path` in the resolved fields list (currently absent).
11	
12	- **Ecosystem-check [I] Install and [A] Abort exit silently — no exit code, no result envelope** | src/skf-quick-skill/references/ecosystem-check.md:62-63
13	  Issue: Both terminal branches "Display install instructions, then end workflow" and "Display 'Compilation cancelled.', then end workflow" omit any exit code or HARD HALT envelope. Headless automators that hit an ecosystem match and the gate auto-resolves to `[P]` proceed are fine, but human-driven [I]/[A] paths return with no envelope on stderr, no `quick-skill-result-latest.json` on disk (since `{skill_package}` is unknown at this phase), and exit code 0 implying success — contradicting the SKILL.md "Exit Codes" promise that "Every HARD HALT in this workflow exits with a stable, documented code."
14	  Fix: Add an exit code for `ecosystem-cancelled` (or fold into `user-cancelled` at a new code 2 / 6 — pick one), and emit the HARD HALT stderr envelope per SKILL.md "Result Contract on HARD HALT" with `phase: "ecosystem-check"`, `skill_package: null`, before the workflow ends. Update the Exit Codes table accordingly.
15	
16	## Findings by Dimension
17	
18	### Architecture (structural integrity, prose craft, cohesion)
19	
20	- **[A1] Language-header style inconsistent with Excellent peer** | severity: low | src/skf-quick-skill/references/*.md (top of each file)
21	  Issue: Every stage opens with a visible body line `Communicate with the user in \`{communication_language}\`.` while the Excellent peer (`skf-brief-skill/references/analyze-target.md:9`) uses an invisible `<!-- Config: communicate in {communication_language}. -->` HTML comment that doesn't render to the user but still seeds the LLM. Stages that produce written artifacts also need `{document_output_language}` (compile, write-and-validate, finalize handle this; others should too where appropriate).
22	  Fix: Replace the body-line directives with HTML-comment headers; add `{document_output_language}` mention to the comment in stages that emit user-facing prose.
23	
24	- **[A2] Ecosystem-check is over-ceremonied for the work it does** | severity: medium | src/skf-quick-skill/references/ecosystem-check.md
25	  Issue: A trivial advisory query/match/no-match check (88 lines) carries two `Menu Handling Logic` blocks + two `EXECUTION RULES` blocks + a `CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE` for what amounts to "if match, ask; otherwise proceed." The Menu Handling Logic for §4 ("After no-match or skip determination, immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}") and its EXECUTION RULES are pure ceremony around a one-line auto-proceed.
26	  Fix: Collapse §3 and §4 into a single decision branch; drop the §4 Menu Handling Logic + EXECUTION RULES blocks; trim from 88 → ~50 lines. Resolves an "earn its keep" violation called out in the principles file ("multiple files that could be a single instruction" / "defensive padding").
27	
28	- **[A3] SKILL.md Result Contract schema table duplicates partial content already in shared/references/output-contract-schema.md** | severity: low | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:100-112
29	  Issue: The 13-line schema table is faithfully Quick-Skill-specific but a near-duplicate of what `src/shared/references/output-contract-schema.md` already documents. Since SKILL.md already runs 233 lines (within ceiling but at the top of the multi-branch range), one option is to point at the shared doc plus a Quick-Skill-specific delta paragraph for the `error.code` enum and `skill_package: null` semantics.
30	  Fix: Either keep as-is (load-bearing for a HARD-HALT-heavy workflow) or extract `error.*` field rows to a `references/result-contract.md` carved-out. Recommend keep-as-is unless SKILL.md line-budget pressure increases — the schema is referenced from 7 stage HARD HALT branches and survives compaction better when inline. Mark as a deferred decision rather than a fix.
31	
32	- **[A4] Batch Mode lives at SKILL.md scope but is a discrete sub-protocol** | severity: low | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:137-232
33	  Issue: ~95 lines of Batch Mode + its boundary-event JSON sub-schema sit at SKILL.md scope. Single-target users (the common case) pay weight for this on every activation. Carving Batch Mode to `references/batch-mode.md` and replacing the SKILL.md section with a 3-4-line summary + path pointer would cut SKILL.md from 233 → ~140 lines.
34	  Fix: Carve `references/batch-mode.md` with input format, execution loop, summary contract, headless events, and exit code; leave a short pointer block in SKILL.md. Survives compaction because On Activation step 4 explicitly loads the file when `--batch` is set.
35	
36	- **[A5] Workflow Rules over-rules — duplication with implicit conventions** | severity: low | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:24-39
37	  Issue: Lines 28-29 ("Read each step file completely before taking any action" / "Follow the mandatory sequence in each step exactly — do not skip, reorder, or optimize" / "Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps") are LLM-native defaults already covered by the chain-via-`nextStepFile` mechanism; they don't prevent a failure mode the model would otherwise hit. The principles file calls these out as "Numbered procedural steps for things the LLM does naturally."
38	  Fix: Drop the three load/order rules; keep only "Never fabricate content — all data must come from source extraction or user input" (load-bearing for hallucination prevention), `{communication_language}`, and the two headless-mode lines (load-bearing for headless contract).
39	
40	### Determinism (intelligence placement, parallelization, script opportunities)
41	
42	- **[D1] Customize-resolver activation step is missing** | severity: medium | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:116-135 (On Activation)
43	  Issue: The Excellent peer (`skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md:46-67`) runs `python3 {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_customization.py --skill {skill-root} --key workflow` on activation and applies a path-scalar fallback so stage files reference `{<purpose>_path}` directly. Quick-skill skips this — there is no `customize.toml` and no resolver call. Since C1 (below) proposes lifting the skill-template asset, registries data, and batch output to scalars, the resolver step needs to land alongside.
44	  Fix: Add a customization resolver step (mirror `skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md:46-67`) once a `customize.toml` is introduced — this is gated on C1 below. Token cost: zero (one batched activation script call replaces in-prompt path defaulting).
45	
46	- **[D2] Prepass flags 3 sequential tool-call steps in SKILL.md — all are genuine data dependencies** | severity: low | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:116, 133, 135
47	  Issue: `execution-deps-prepass.json` flags 3 sequential tool-call steps. Inspection: step 1 already correctly batches config + preferences ("one batched tool-call message — they are independent files"), but step 4 (`--batch` → read batch file) and step 5 (load `resolve-target.md`) are unavoidable serial dependencies on prior values. The prepass count is true but the steps are genuinely sequential — no parallelisation opportunity. The meaningful win is the parallel-fetch directive in `quick-extract.md:26` (README + manifest + entry-point batched as one message).
48	  Fix: Leave activation as-is. Tracked here so future reviewers don't re-raise the prepass false positive.
49	
50	- **[D3] §1 README read + §2 source-files fetch in quick-extract aren't quite as parallel as the directive claims** | severity: low | src/skf-quick-skill/references/quick-extract.md:26, 28-29, 64-79
51	  Issue: The parallel-fetch directive at line 26 instructs README + manifest + entry-point as one batched message, but §1 also covers extraction from README content (description, features, usage, installation) — meaning the LLM reads §1 content *after* the parallel fetch returns. That ordering is fine for execution but the directive's claim "one batched tool-call message rather than three sequential round trips" needs to be matched by a sub-step that explicitly states "fetch in one message, then parse the responses." Today's prose reads as though parsing-extraction happens inside the fetch step.
52	  Fix: Rewrite §1 + §2 into a single "Fetch in parallel, then parse" subsection that lists the URLs to fetch in one message and then walks the extraction in §1.x / §2.x.
53	
54	- **[D4] Multi-module Maven / Gradle loop is serial in prose despite a parallel sub-module fetch directive** | severity: low | src/skf-quick-skill/references/quick-extract.md:79, 107
55	  Issue: Line 79 says "loop §2+§3 per module. Sub-module fetches are safe to issue as one batched tool-call message — N module fetches collapse to O(1) wall-clock time." Line 107 then says "fetch each sub-module's manifest + entry-point files (§2) and re-invoke the helper per module (§3), aggregating exports[] across all module envelopes." The "re-invoke the helper per module" is serial — each module's helper invocation needs its own subprocess. If `skf-extract-public-api.py` accepts arbitrarily many `entries` (line 94), the per-module loop could collapse to one helper call with all sub-module entries (loses the per-module package_name, gains throughput).
56	  Fix: Verify whether `skf-extract-public-api.py` supports per-module aggregation in a single call; if yes, document the collapse. If no, keep the per-module helper loop but call out that the wall-clock saving is fetch-side, not helper-invocation-side.
57	
58	### Customization Surface (customize.toml)
59	
60	- **[C1] No customize.toml at all — at least three lift-worthy paths** | severity: high-opportunity | src/skf-quick-skill/ (missing customize.toml)
61	  Issue: Quick-skill ships no `customize.toml`. Three immediate lift candidates exist:
62	  - `assets/skill-template.md` → `skill_template_path` (referenced from compile.md:3, 27)
63	  - `references/registry-resolution.md` → `registry_resolution_path` (referenced from resolve-target.md:3, 84 — fallback when shared resolver script is absent)
64	  - `{skills_output_folder}/_batch/` → `batch_output_path` (orgs may want batch summaries to land in a different folder, e.g., `~/skf-batch/`)
65	  Plus the BMad-default `persistent_facts = ["file:{project-root}/**/project-context.md"]` for orgs that want house-style enforcement. The principles file calls this out: workflow has 2+ hardcoded asset paths and no `customize.toml` at all → high-opportunity to opt in.
66	  Fix: Create `customize.toml` mirroring `skf-brief-skill/customize.toml` shape (header, `[workflow]` block, BMad-default `persistent_facts`, three optional path scalars). Add the resolver step to On Activation per D1. Update compile.md / resolve-target.md / batch-summary write logic to reference `{skillTemplatePath}` / `{registryResolutionPath}` / `{batchOutputPath}`. Add the activation-time path-scalar fallback (empty-string override falls through to bundled default).
67	
68	- **[C2] Six CLI override flags would benefit from documentation persistence** | severity: low-opportunity | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:124-131
69	  Issue: The override flags (`--description`, `--exports`, `--skip-snippet`, `--no-active-pointer`, `--batch`, `--fail-fast`) are CLI-only — orgs can't pre-set defaults across all runs. A team that always wants `--no-active-pointer` (because they orchestrate pointer flips downstream) must remember to pass the flag every time.
70	  Fix: Optional — `[workflow.defaults]` block in customize.toml carrying the default flag values, merged into `{overrides}` at activation. Defer; the override surface is reasonable as-is and the flags are op-specific.
71	
72	### Enhancement (UX gaps, headless contract, edge cases, facilitative patterns)
73	
74	- **[E1] No cancel-line affordance on interactive gates** | severity: medium-opportunity | src/skf-quick-skill/references/resolve-target.md:33-40, ecosystem-check.md:50-55, compile.md:148-150
75	  Issue: Brief-skill's gather-intent.md (line 96, 102) tells users they can type `cancel` / `exit` / `[X]` / `q` / `:q` at any prompt to exit cleanly with a `user-cancelled` exit code. Quick-skill's prompts have menu choices ([P]/[I]/[A], [C]/[E]/[S]/[Q]) but no universal cancel — a user who wanted to abort during the §1 Resolve Target prompt has no documented escape; the prompt waits indefinitely. The closest is [Q] at the compile step (exit code 6).
76	  Fix: Document a `cancel` / `exit` / `:q` universal cancel-line affordance in SKILL.md `Workflow Rules` and the §1 Resolve Target prompt at minimum. Wire to exit code 6 (compile-cancelled) or add a new exit code 2 (user-cancelled, matching brief-skill's exit code 6 mapping). Resolves the brief-skill peer-feature parity gap.
77	
78	- **[E2] Activation lacks a forge_data_folder / skills_output_folder write probe** | severity: medium-opportunity | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:116-135
79	  Issue: Brief-skill `gather-intent.md:34-40` runs a single-byte write-and-remove probe to `{forge_data_folder}` at the top of step 1 — catches read-only-mount / disk-full / permission-denied paths before the user invests 5–15 minutes in interactive scoping. Quick-skill skips this; on a quick interactive run the user invests 1-2 minutes on resolve+ecosystem before write-failure in step 5. A 5-second pre-flight write probe would shift the failure earlier with no UX cost.
80	  Fix: Add a pre-flight write probe to `{skills_output_folder}` (since that's where step 5 §1 writes) inside On Activation, mirror the brief-skill pattern. HALT with exit code 4 (write-failure) and the standard envelope if it fails.
81	
82	- **[E3] Open-floor opening hint missing — straight to "Provide a target"** | severity: low-opportunity | src/skf-quick-skill/references/resolve-target.md:30-40
83	  Issue: The principles file's "Open-floor opening" pattern is reserved for conversational skills with scoping decisions to make. Quick-skill is intentionally non-conversational ("speed is the priority"), so a strict open-floor would dilute its identity. But a minimal hint — "If you have a language hint or scope hint up front, include them now to skip the multi-language gate later" — would help expert users who know their target multi-language repo will trip the §4 gate.
84	  Fix: Defer or add a one-line hint to the §1 Resolve Target prompt. Low priority — Quick-Skill's value prop is "no brief needed," not "guided discovery."
85	
86	- **[E4] Progress event slug list duplicates the Stages table** | severity: low | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:34-39
87	  Issue: The bullet list of progress-event slugs (`resolve-target`, `ecosystem-check`, ...) at line 39 duplicates the Stages table at line 43-51. If the table is the source of truth, the slug list ages.
88	  Fix: Replace line 39 with "see the Stages table — `<slug>` is the kebab portion of the filename" (one-line, single source of truth).
89	
90	- **[E5] No mention of recovery suggestion in HARD HALT envelopes** | severity: low-opportunity | src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md:101-112
91	  Issue: The completion summary in finalize.md:67-73 suggests `test-skill` and `export-skill` after success, but the HARD HALT envelopes carry no analogue — automators reading `quick-skill-result-latest.json` see `{status: "error", ...}` but no suggested-recovery field. Brief-skill emits an `error_code` enum that downstream tools can branch on; quick-skill emits `error.code` too, but no suggestion ladder ("re-run with --language-hint", "try /skf-create-skill from a brief").
92	  Fix: Optional. Add an `error.suggestion` field carrying a one-line recovery hint per error-code class. Low ROI but a delight-tier improvement for chained-pipeline UX.
93	
94	## Deterministic Summary
95	
96	- **path-standards:** 0 findings across 10 files (no double-prefix, no bare bmad, no absolute paths, no relative-prefix). Baseline-Excellent on path hygiene.
97	- **scripts:** 0 findings — no `scripts/` directory exists locally; all deterministic helpers live in `src/shared/scripts/` and are probe-order-resolved from stage frontmatter. Healthy — no per-skill duplication.
98	- **workflow-integrity:** 0 issues. 15 sections detected in SKILL.md; stage-summary shows zero stages parsed (the prepass parser doesn't recognize the `nextStepFile` frontmatter chain pattern as "stages" — prepass limitation, not a defect). Workflow type correctly classified as `simple-workflow`.
99	- **prompt-metrics:** SKILL.md at 233 lines / 3689 tokens, 6 tables, 7 fenced blocks, 15 sections — within "Multi-branch SKILL.md: up to ~250 lines" target. Two largest references: `compile.md` (172/2269) and `quick-extract.md` (180/2946). No waste patterns, no back-references. Pre-pass health: passes.
100	- **execution-deps:** 1 medium issue — "3 sequential tool-call steps." Inspection shows genuine data dependencies (config-read → batch-file-read → first-stage-load); not a parallelisation miss. See D2.
101	
102	## Strengths (preserve)
103	
104	- **Probe-order helper resolution** with installed-first / dev-fallback ordering — `packageResolverProbeOrder`, `publicApiExtractorProbeOrder`, `quickMetadataRendererProbeOrder`, `frontmatterValidatorProbeOrder`, `outputValidatorProbeOrder`, `atomicWriteProbeOrder` — every fragile-operation invocation is exact, with one right way (principles "When Procedure IS Value" section).
105	- **Ref-aware source reading** in quick-extract.md:24 — `?ref={source_ref}` propagates the user-specified `@version` through every fetch so the metadata claim matches the extracted source. Catches a quiet provenance bug that bare GitHub-content reads would silently hide.
106	- **Repo-Shape Sniff** in quick-extract.md:40-62 — graceful-degradation classifier for awesome-list / docs-site / examples-only repos, with a soft-warn gate and headless-mode auto-proceed that surfaces the signal in the result contract. Catches "wrap a library" misapplications before they produce a degenerate SKILL.md.
107	- **Paired success+error result contracts** — every HARD HALT path emits an `SKF_QUICK_SKILL_RESULT_JSON: {...}` stderr envelope with a stable exit code, and post-step-5 HALTs additionally write the `-latest.json` to disk. Headless automators never see a missing `-latest.json` after a failed run.
108	- **Batch Mode with per-target boundary events + batch summary contract** — the `{"batch":<n>,"target":...}` boundary events and the `partial`/`failed`/`success` resolution rule are well-designed for CI consumers and the future `skf-batch-skills` meta-workflow.
109	- **Headless coercion logic** — `--batch` implies `--headless` with explicit log entry "headless: coerced by --batch"; pre-empts the "headless flag silently overrode my intent" footgun.
110	- **Atomic active-pointer flip via shared helper** — explicit ban on `rm + ln -s` with two named failure modes, Windows junction fallback, locked via `flock` on `active.skf-lock`. Production-grade infrastructure for what looks like a "just symlink it" operation.
111	- **Zero-Exports Soft Gate (rescue mode)** at quick-extract.md:130-151 — catches the degenerate-SKILL.md case before write, offers retry-with-hints / proceed / abort, headless-mode auto-proceed surfaces the signal. Facilitative pattern the principles file calls "capture-don't-interrupt."
112	
113	## Recommendations (top 3 ranked by impact)
114	
115	1. **Fix `{forger_root}` → `{sidecar_path}` and add ecosystem-check exit envelopes** — resolves 2 broken-section findings, effort: low. This is the only real runtime bug and the only contract gap; both fixes are tiny.
116	
117	2. **Introduce customize.toml with three path scalars + resolver activation step** — resolves [C1] + [D1], effort: low-medium. Mirror the `skf-brief-skill/customize.toml` shape, lift `skillTemplatePath` / `registryResolutionPath` / `batchOutputPath`, ship BMad-default `persistent_facts`. Adds the customize-resolver step to On Activation. This is the "Foundation" half of the Foundation+Tightening pattern that earned Excellent in #306.
118	
119	3. **Tightening pass** — resolves [A1] (HTML-comment language headers), [A2] (collapse ecosystem-check ceremony), [A5] (drop redundant workflow rules), [E1] (cancel-line affordance), [E2] (write-probe), [E4] (slug list dedup), effort: medium. This is the "Tightening" half of the pattern from #323. Together with #1 and #2, brings quick-skill to par with the post-elevation Core skills.
120

1	# Quality Findings: skf-create-stack-skill
2	
3	**Grade:** Good
4	**Narrative:** The skill has unusually deep institutional substance — atomic-write rollback contract, file-list-intersection prune, exports resolution order, compose-mode co-mention precision guards, confidence-tier inheritance matrix — and a clean SKILL.md shape that mirrors the Excellent peers. The gap to Excellent is concentrated in two areas: zero customization surface (no `customize.toml`, no `{document_output_language}` plumbing for the artifacts that get persisted, no asset path lift candidates exposed despite 3+ asset-shaped paths) and a lifecycle/headless contract that is half-built (per-step error envelopes are well-specified but no Exit Codes table, no top-line `SKF_*_RESULT_JSON` envelope shape, and no `[X]` cancel option on the two interactive gates that the B6 cancel-lines pattern from the elevated peers requires).
5	
6	## Broken (critical/high)
7	
8	- **Cross-directory `./` reference in compile-stack.md** | `src/skf-create-stack-skill/references/compile-stack.md:77`
9	  Issue: `Link to reference file: \`./references/{library}.md\`` — `./` resolves to the *same folder* (i.e. `references/`), which would point at `references/references/{library}.md`. In the generated stack-skill artifact the per-library reference lives at `references/{library}.md` from the skill root. The template should emit a bare/anchored path, not `./references/`.
10	  Fix: change the emitted link template to `references/{library}.md` (bare from generated skill root). Also fix the matching pattern in `assets/stack-skill-template.md:35` which shows `[ref](./references/{name}.md)` — same bug propagating into every generated stack.
11	
12	- **Bare `_bmad/` reference in health-check.md frontmatter comment** | `src/skf-create-stack-skill/references/health-check.md:3`
13	  Issue: `# (\`_bmad/skf/\` when installed, \`src/\` during development), NOT relative` — bare `_bmad` without `{project-root}` prefix per the path-standards scanner.
14	  Fix: write as `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/` so the comment matches the project-wide path convention (the actual `nextStepFile: 'shared/health-check.md'` resolver lives in module-loader logic; the comment is the only user-facing artifact and should use the canonical prefix).
15	
16	## Findings by Dimension
17	
18	### Architecture (structural integrity, prose craft, cohesion)
19	
20	- **[A1] No customize.toml present** | severity: high | `src/skf-create-stack-skill/` (missing file)
21	  Issue: principles file says workflow-shape conventions ship `customize.toml` with `activation_steps_prepend/append`, `persistent_facts`, and any workflow scalars; the Excellent peer `skf-brief-skill/customize.toml` is the calibration shape. This skill has none — overrides require forking.
22	  Fix: ship `customize.toml` with the standard `[workflow]` block + `persistent_facts = ["file:{project-root}/**/project-context.md"]` + scalar lifts for the 3 asset paths (see C1). Wire SKILL.md `## On Activation` to call the bmad-customize resolver script (peer pattern).
23	
24	- **[A2] Description trigger phrase incomplete** | severity: medium | `src/skf-create-stack-skill/SKILL.md:3`
25	  Issue: principles say "Use when user says 'specific phrase' or 'specific phrase'" — current text quotes only one phrase (`"create a stack skill."`). Real users say multiple things (stack the project, forge a stack, build a stack skill).
26	  Fix: extend to 2-3 quoted phrases per peer pattern (e.g., `Use when the user requests to "create a stack skill", "forge a stack", or "stack this project."`).
27	
28	- **[A3] `[workflow]` identity prose in `## Role`** | severity: low | `src/skf-create-stack-skill/SKILL.md:22`
29	  Issue: "operating in Ferris Architect mode" is an agent-persona phrasing that does not earn its keep (principles: "Identity / communication-style / principles in [workflow] → workflow wants to be an agent"). The "dependency analyst and integration architect" role is load-bearing; the Ferris-mode tag is noise.
30	  Fix: drop the "operating in Ferris Architect mode" clause. Keep the analyst+integration-architect framing.
31

119	## Deterministic Summary
120	- **path-standards:** 2 high findings — (1) `./references/{library}.md` cross-dir-dot-slash in compile-stack.md:77 (propagates into the generated artifact via assets template), (2) bare `_bmad/skf/` in health-check.md:3 comment.
121	- **scripts:** 0 findings (no scripts/ dir found — but D1/D2/D5 flag this as a high-value gap, not a clean pass).
122	- **workflow-integrity:** pass — 0 issues. Note: prepass reports `total_stages: 0` because the workflow uses frontmatter `nextStepFile` chaining rather than the named-stage convention the prepass detects. The Stages table in SKILL.md and Step-N labels in references map cleanly.
123	- **prompt-metrics:** SKILL.md 73 lines / 1225 tokens (well under the 130 ceiling); largest reference is generate-output.md at 329 lines / 3428 tokens (lift candidate — see D4). Two other heavy references: detect-integrations.md 165 lines / 3402 tokens, parallel-extract.md 178 lines / 2511 tokens — both lift-worthy if customization/scripts trim them further.
124	- **execution-deps:** pass — 0 issues. The dependency graph between steps is linear via `nextStepFile` chaining; no parallel-group claims and no over-constraint. The parallelization opportunity is intra-step (per-library extraction in step 4, per-pair grep in step 5).
125	
126	## Strengths (preserve)
127	- Atomic-write contract with stage-dir / commit-dir / flip-link / rollback (`generate-output.md` §1-9) — institutional rigor most skills lack.
128	- File-list intersection as MANDATORY first pass in pair detection (`detect-integrations.md` §1) — turns 210 prescribed greps into ~12 for typical N≈21 codebases.
129	- Exports resolution order (H1) in `parallel-extract.md` §0 — three explicit fallback layers with warning logging.
130	- Compose-mode co-mention precision guards (H2 — word-boundary + section filtering + 2-paragraph minimum) — exactly the kind of "make heuristic detection less wrong" rigor that earns its keep.
131	- Confidence Tier Inheritance matrix in `compose-mode-rules.md` — covers all 6 pair combinations with audit-friendly suffixes.
132	- `workflow_warnings[]` accumulator pattern (SKILL.md Workflow Rules) — single sink, structured shape, surfaced at step 9 §5.
133	- Schema-version guard on feasibility-report consumption (`detect-integrations.md` §2, `compose-mode-rules.md` Feasibility Report Integration) — refuses to interpret unknown versions.
134	- Headless precedence resolver (B2) with explicit-disable / explicit-enable / preferences / default ordering — peer-quality.
135	- Top-K cap at 20 with explicit warning + record in evidence report (S7) — graceful degradation done right.
136	
137	## Recommendations (top 3 ranked by impact)
138	
139	1. **Ship `customize.toml` + thread `{document_output_language}` through artifact-producing steps** — resolves C1, C2, C3, and unblocks the broader "headless automator" story by making the surface inspectable. Effort: medium (one new file + ~6 prompt insertions). This is the single largest gap to Excellent.
140	
141	2. **Add Exit Codes table + top-level `SKF_STACK_RESULT_JSON:` stdout envelope + `[X] Cancel` option on both interactive gates** — resolves A4, E1, E2, and aligns the headless contract surface 1:1 with the elevated peer. Effort: medium. Without this, headless automators can't branch cleanly on failures and interactive users have no abort path.
142	
143	3. **Lift 2-3 deterministic operations to `scripts/`** — at minimum `scan-manifests.py` (D2) + `enumerate-skills.py` (compose-mode discovery with the existing export-manifest/symlink/cycle/hash logic) + `pair-intersect.py` (D5). Resolves D1, D2, D5; trims detect-manifests.md and detect-integrations.md by ~50% each; replaces N-skill SKILL.md reads with one script invocation. Effort: medium-high (3 PEP 723 scripts + JSON contracts + prompt rewrites). This is what makes the determinism story credible at the Excellent bar.
144

1	# Quality Findings: skf-analyze-source
2	
3	**Grade:** Good
4	**Narrative:** A well-structured, multi-stage discovery workflow with clean stage chaining, a thoughtful tier-aware analysis model, and a serviceable result contract. To reach Excellent it needs the same Foundation+Tightening treatment given to skf-brief-skill: a customize.toml surface, an explicit headless Result Contract / Exit Codes block in SKILL.md, B6-style cancel-lines on the three confirm gates, and `{communication_language}` / `{document_output_language}` wiring inside every reference. There is also one path-standards violation and one orphaned forward-reference (subprocess Patterns) that the LLM cannot resolve at runtime.

Fix: rewrite the comment to refer to `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/` (or rephrase to avoid the path entirely — the comment is explanatory only).

The LLM has no patterns file to consult, so the instruction is a noop at best and a confused tangent at worst. Fix: either inline what the "pattern" means (a per-unit subagent fan-out vs. a single-process scan) in the rule itself, or drop the Pattern N labels.

Each subagent returns ONLY: `{unit_name, files_count, exports_count, export_pattern, api_surface, scripts_assets, ccc_signals, strategy_used, confidence}`. No prose." Instruct the parent not to read unit source files before delegating (implicit-read trap).

50	  Issue: Several operations in stages 02-04 are pure plumbing the LLM is paying tokens to perform:
51	  - manifest discovery (find package.json / Cargo.toml / pyproject.toml / go.mod across `project_paths[]`) — a 30-line python script with `pathlib.Path.rglob` returns structured JSON.
52	  - disqualification counts (file count < 3, LoC < 100, generated-code detection by `node_modules/`, `vendor/`, `dist/` heuristics) — fully deterministic.
53	  - import-graph extraction for Forge/Deep tiers — even a regex-grade pass for Quick tier saves ~5-10k tokens per unit.
54	  - stack-skill co-import frequency (count of cross-unit imports across 3+ files) — pure counting.

1	# Quality Findings: skf-audit-skill
2	
3	**Grade:** Good
4	**Narrative:** Structurally solid — SKILL.md is lean (68 lines), routing is intact, frontmatter chains cleanly, and the workflow's load-bearing institutional knowledge (manifest-vs-symlink drift gate, dirty-worktree probe, hash-prefix normalization, extractor canonicalization) is well-earned text that prevents real failure modes seen in commits #302/#312. To clear the Excellent bar, the skill needs the customization surface every peer SKF skill ships (no `customize.toml`), the headless contract every Excellent peer surfaces (no Exit Codes table, no `SKF_AUDIT_RESULT_JSON` stdout envelope), language-header consistency across carved files, and one path-standards fix.

10	  Fix: prepend `{project-root}/` to both path mentions inside the comment.

17	  Issue: principles file requires each carved file to use `{communication_language}` (and `{document_output_language}` where it produces a doc) so it survives context compaction standalone. SKILL.md:34 sets the rule once but no reference file restates or uses the variable. The drift report itself is written user-facing — `{document_output_language}` is the right knob for it.
18	  Fix: add a one-line "Always communicate in `{communication_language}`; drift report prose in `{document_output_language}`" rule at the top of each reference file (or in the per-step `## Rules` block).

The workflow actually consumes a half-dozen inputs in init.md (full-path override, tier override via preferences.yaml, degraded-mode opt-in, upstream-drift gate choice C/S/X, dirty-worktree sub-gate T/A/F). Headless callers and the automator archetype have no documented surface for pre-supplying these answers — they default-resolve silently.
22	  Fix: enumerate the optional inputs (`skill_path`, `tier_override`, `degraded`, `upstream_drift_choice`, `dirty_worktree_choice`, `force`) in the Inputs row so headless invocations are first-class. Mirror brief-skill's contract shape (src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md:86).

This is duplicated in every step file's "Pattern N if available, else main thread" wording and overrides them weakly. Real fallback decisions are step-specific (e.g. structural-diff needs sequential iteration, semantic-diff short-circuits at non-Deep).
26	  Fix: drop the blanket clause; per-step fallback wording already covers it.

28	### Determinism (intelligence placement, parallelization, script opportunities)
29	
30	- **[D1] Hash comparison and prefix normalization belong in a script** | severity: medium | references/structural-diff.md:86-100 (§4b Script/Asset Drift)
31	  Issue: For each file in `provenance-map.file_entries`, the LLM is told to compute SHA-256 of the source file, strip the algorithm prefix off the stored hash (per the §4b note that fixed the original false-positive bug), and compare. This is pure plumbing — identical input → identical output, unit-testable. At even modest skill sizes (20+ tracked file_entries) the LLM tax for hashing prose alone is meaningful, plus the LLM has no native SHA-256 — it has to shell out per file regardless.
32	  Fix: ship `scripts/compare-file-hashes.py {provenance-map.json} {source-root}` that returns a compact JSON of `{added: [], removed: [], changed: [{path, stored_hash, current_hash}]}`. The LLM consumes the JSON; the prefix-stripping logic and the writer-vs-reader compatibility note (currently load-bearing prose) move into the script and stop being a hazard.
33	
34	- **[D2] Pre-pass opportunity: provenance-map normalization** | severity: medium | references/init.md:79-122 (loading provenance map, detecting stack skill, resolving source path) + references/structural-diff.md:32-44 (canonicalization transforms)
35	  Issue: init.md and structural-diff.md both walk the provenance map to extract: `entries[].source_file`, `file_entries[].source_file`, `provenance_version`, `skill_type`, top-level `libraries` key, `__init__.py` re-export map. Then structural-diff.md re-walks to apply three canonicalization transforms (quote-style, stdlib module qualification, public-API re-export). The bounded scan list, the stack-skill flags, and the re-export map are all deterministic projections.
36	  Fix: a small pre-pass script (`scripts/load-provenance.py`) that returns `{bounded_scan_files: [...], is_stack_skill, legacy_stack_provenance, source_root, baseline_commit, baseline_ref, reexport_map: {...}}` would replace ~80 lines of LLM walk-and-extract prose with one read of a JSON.

The current fallback wording ("Perform extraction in main thread, processing each file sequentially") locks in sequential when a batched-Read pass would be free.
40	  Fix: amend the fallback line: "Perform extraction in main thread, batching Read calls across files in one message where the bounded scan list permits."

Without it: (a) `persistent_facts` discovery (the BMad default `["file:{project-root}/**/project-context.md"]`) is unreachable; (b) the drift-report template (`assets/drift-report-template.md`) is not overrideable, so an org wanting house-styled drift reports must fork the skill; (c) `activation_steps_prepend` / `activation_steps_append` for org-wide pre-flight (compliance, auth) is unavailable.

Wire the scalar in On Activation via `python3 {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_customization.py --skill {skill-root} --key workflow`, matching the brief-skill pattern at src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md:47-67.

The file is referenced as `severityRulesFile` frontmatter scalar in severity-classify.md but cannot be redirected via customize.toml. Teams wanting stricter or looser drift thresholds must fork.
50	  Fix: when adding `customize.toml` per C1, also expose `severity_rules_path = ""` (default fallback to `references/severity-rules.md`). Low-opportunity because severity tables are stable institutional knowledge; flag it only because the seam is already present in the frontmatter.

55	  Issue: The Excellent peer brief-skill (src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md:93-104) ships a stable Exit Codes table so headless automators can branch on failure class. audit-skill has half a dozen HARD HALT paths (skill not found at resolved path, setup-forge not run, manifest-vs-symlink user abort, upstream-drift user abort, dirty-worktree user abort, source dir missing) that currently emit free-text prose and no exit code. Pipelines wrapping `audit-skill` cannot distinguish "no setup forge" from "user aborted upstream gate" without grepping.
56	  Fix: add an Exit Codes table (success=0; input-missing=2; resolution-failure=3 for missing forge-tier.yaml / missing source dir; user-cancelled=6 for X selections at the upstream-drift / dirty-worktree / baseline gates). Mirror brief-skill's table shape.

59	  Issue: report.md writes a result JSON to disk at `{forge_version}/audit-skill-result-{timestamp}.json` (+ `-latest.json` copy), but does not emit a single-line `SKF_AUDIT_RESULT_JSON: {...}` envelope on stdout. Brief-skill (src/skf-brief-skill/SKILL.md:106-114) and update-skill both ship the envelope so a chaining workflow can read drift_score + report_path from a captured stdout line without filesystem polling.
60	  Fix: emit `SKF_AUDIT_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"success|error","skill_name":"…","drift_score":"CLEAN|MINOR|SIGNIFICANT|CRITICAL","report_path":"…","next_workflow":"update-skill|null","audit_ref":"…","exit_code":0,"halt_reason":null}` on stdout when `{headless_mode}` is true (and mirror-on-stderr on HALT, per the brief-skill pattern). Add it to report.md §5 alongside the on-disk Result Contract.

63	  Issue: Drift reports are timestamped (`drift-report-{timestamp}.md`) and the workflow always creates a new one. There's no detection of an existing recent report for the same skill — a user who re-runs after fixing one finding gets a fresh report with no diff against the prior one. The principles file's Decision-Log Workspace pattern would surface "skill X was last audited 3 days ago at SIGNIFICANT — re-audit, diff against prior report, or compare?" The drift report itself is appended-to across steps and has frontmatter (`stepsCompleted`, `drift_score`, `nextWorkflow`) — it's already a workspace artifact in shape, just missing the resume gesture.
64	  Fix: at init.md §6, before creating the new report, glob `{forge_version}/drift-report-*.md` and if a recent (< 7 days) report exists for the same `audit_ref`, surface a soft gate: `[F] Fresh audit (default) / [D] Diff against prior report / [R] Resume the prior report (recover incomplete run)`. Headless default = F.
