```
1	---
2	name: skf-brief-skill
3	description: Design a skill scope through guided discovery. Use when the user requests to "create a skill brief" or "brief a skill".
4	---
5	
6	# Brief Skill
7	
8	## Overview
9	
10	Helps the user define what to skill — target repo, scope, language, inclusion/exclusion patterns — and produces a `skill-brief.yaml` that drives create-skill. This is the first step in the skill creation pipeline; the brief is the input contract for create-skill, which performs the actual compilation.
11	
12	A good skill brief sets a tight, cohesive boundary: one capability with 3-8 primary functions, an unambiguous public API surface, and a description short enough to fit in a registry row. Briefs that try to cover several unrelated concerns (e.g. authentication *and* data visualization) compile into skills that no agent can route to confidently — a brief covering too much is a worse failure mode than a brief covering too little, and this workflow steers toward the smaller, sharper version when scope is unclear.
13	
14	Brief-skill is split from create-skill so the scoping conversation runs *once*, on cheap signals (manifests, top-level exports, intent), without paying for AST extraction. Compilation is expensive; scoping decisions are cheap to revise. Keeping them in separate workflows lets a user iterate on the brief, share it for review, and re-run create-skill against the same brief whenever the upstream version moves.
15	
16	## Conventions
17	
18	- Bare paths (e.g. `references/<name>.md`) resolve from the skill root.
19	- `references/` holds prompt content carved out of SKILL.md (workflow stages chained via frontmatter `nextStepFile`, plus static reference docs); `scripts/` and `assets/` hold deterministic helpers and templates.
20	- `{skill-root}` resolves to this skill's installed directory (where `customize.toml` lives, if present).
21	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
22	- `{skill-name}` resolves to the skill directory's basename.
23	
24	## Role
25	
26	You are a skill scoping architect collaborating with a developer who wants to create an agent skill. You bring expertise in source code analysis, API surface identification, and skill boundary design, while the user brings their domain knowledge and specific use case. Work together as equals.
27	
28	## Workflow Rules
29	
30	These rules apply to every step in this workflow:
31	
32	- Read each step file completely before taking any action
33	- Follow the mandatory sequence in each step exactly — do not skip, reorder, or optimize
34	- Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps
35	- **Lazy-load references and assets:** `references/*.md` and `assets/*.md` files are loaded inside the section that needs them, not at step entry. If a section is skipped (e.g. `version-resolution.md` when `{extractPublicApiScript}` already returned a version, `scope-templates.md` for the `docs-only` branch that bypasses §2c), do not load that file. Each unnecessary load costs context (~5-10 KB per reference) and biases the LLM toward consulting material the current path does not need.
36	- Always communicate in `{communication_language}` (the language for user-facing prose). Written artifact text — the `description`, `notes`, and other free-form fields persisted into `skill-brief.yaml` — is in `{document_output_language}`; per-step rules call this out where it applies (see step 5). The two values may be the same.
37	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
38	
39	## On Activation
40	
41	1. Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/config.yaml` and resolve:
42	   - `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `forge_data_folder`, `sidecar_path`
43	
44	2. **Resolve `{headless_mode}`**: true if `--headless` or `-H` was passed as an argument, or if `headless_mode: true` in preferences.yaml. Default: false.
45	
46	3. **Resolve workflow customization.** Run:
47	
48	   ```bash
49	   python3 {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_customization.py \
50	       --skill {skill-root} --key workflow
51	   ```
52	
53	   The script merges the three customization layers per `bmad-customize`'s structural merge rules (scalars override, arrays append):
54	
55	   - `{skill-root}/customize.toml` — bundled defaults
56	   - `_bmad/custom/<skill-name>.toml` under `{project-root}` — team overrides (committed)
57	   - `_bmad/custom/<skill-name>.user.toml` under `{project-root}` — personal overrides (gitignored)
58	
59	   If the script fails or is missing, fall back to reading `{skill-root}/customize.toml` directly — the bundled defaults are an empty string for each path scalar.
60	
61	   Apply the path-scalar fallback now so stage files don't have to repeat the conditional logic. For each of the three scalars, if the merged value is empty or absent, use the bundled default:
62	
63	   - `{descriptionVoiceExamplesPath}` ← `workflow.description_voice_examples_path` if non-empty, else `assets/description-voice-examples.md`
64	   - `{scopeTemplatesPath}` ← `workflow.scope_templates_path` if non-empty, else `assets/scope-templates.md`
65	   - `{briefSchemaPath}` ← `workflow.brief_schema_path` if non-empty, else `assets/skill-brief-schema.md`
66	
67	   Stash all three as workflow-context variables. Stage files reference `{descriptionVoiceExamplesPath}` / `{scopeTemplatesPath}` / `{briefSchemaPath}` directly — no conditional at the usage site. Empty-string overrides cleanly fall through to the bundled default; non-empty values let orgs swap in house-style copies without forking the skill.
68	
69	4. Load, read the full file, and execute `references/gather-intent.md`.
70	
71	## Stages
72	
73	| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
74	|---|------|------|--------------|
75	| 1 | Gather Intent | references/gather-intent.md | No (interactive) |
76	| 2 | Analyze Target | references/analyze-target.md | Yes |
77	| 3 | Scope Definition | references/scope-definition.md | No (interactive) |
78	| 4 | Confirm Brief | references/confirm-brief.md | No (confirm) |
79	| 5 | Write Brief | references/write-brief.md | Yes |
80	| 6 | Workflow Health Check (terminal) | references/health-check.md | Yes |
81	
82	## Invocation Contract
83	
84	| Aspect | Detail |
85	|--------|--------|
86	| **Inputs** | `target_repo` [required], `skill_name` [required], `scope_hint` [optional], `language_hint` [optional], `target_version` [optional], `source_authority` [optional: official/community/internal, default community], `source_type` [optional: source/docs-only, default source], `doc_urls` [optional: list of `url[,label]` for source_type=docs-only or supplemental], `scope_type` [optional: full-library/specific-modules/public-api/component-library/reference-app/docs-only], `include` [optional: comma-separated globs], `exclude` [optional: comma-separated globs], `scripts_intent` [optional: detect/none/free-text, default detect], `assets_intent` [optional: detect/none/free-text, default detect], `intent` [optional: free-text used to derive description], `force` [optional: overwrite existing brief without prompting] |
87	| **Gates** | step 1: Input Gate [use args] | step 3: Confirm Gate [C] | step 4: Confirm Gate [C] |
88	| **Outputs** | `skill-brief.yaml` at `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml`; final `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` line on stdout when `{headless_mode}` is true |
89	| **Headless** | All gates auto-resolve with heuristic-driven or default action when `{headless_mode}` is true; pre-supplied inputs consumed at the gates that would otherwise prompt; absent `source_authority` and `scope_type` are resolved by signal-driven detection (see `references/headless-args.md`); existing briefs are preserved unless `--force` was supplied (HALT with `overwrite-cancelled` otherwise) |
90	| **Transient-failure retry** | This workflow does **not** auto-retry network or subprocess failures. A failed `gh` fetch (analyze-target.md §1, portfolio-similarity-check.md), QMD probe, or extraction script is logged and surfaced in the final result envelope as a warning, but the workflow continues with whatever signal it has. Headless pipelines that want retry semantics should wrap the invocation at their orchestrator level (e.g. CI re-runner on non-zero exit). Rationale: brief-skill is read-mostly with one terminal write (the YAML at step 5); a partial-signal retry has more failure modes than just re-running the whole workflow, which is cheap. |
91	| **Exit codes** | See "Exit Codes" below |
92	
93	## Exit Codes
94	
95	Every HARD HALT in this workflow exits with a stable code so headless automators can branch on the failure class without grepping message text:
96	
97	| Code | Meaning              | Raised by                                                                                  |
98	| ---- | -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
99	| 0    | success              | step 6 (terminal)                                                                         |
100	| 2    | input-missing / input-invalid | step 1 GATE — required headless arg absent (`target_repo`, `skill_name`, or `doc_urls` when `source_type=docs-only`) → `input-missing`; enum violation, malformed semver, non-kebab `skill_name`, or step 5 brief-context schema validation failure → `input-invalid` |
101	| 3    | resolution-failure   | step 1 §1 (`forge-tier.yaml` missing); step 2 §1 (target inaccessible / `gh auth` fails) |
102	| 4    | write-failure        | step 1 §1 pre-flight write probe (data folder unwritable: read-only mount, disk full, permissions denied); step 5 §4 (write to `{forge_data_folder}/{skill-name}/skill-brief.yaml` failed) |
103	| 5    | overwrite-cancelled  | step 5 §2 (existing brief, `force` not supplied)                                          |
104	| 6    | user-cancelled       | any interactive menu in step 1/03/04 (user selected `[X]` Cancel and exit)                |
105	
106	## Result Contract (Headless)
107	
108	When `{headless_mode}` is true, step 5 emits a single-line JSON envelope on **stdout** before chaining to step 6, and every HARD HALT emits the same envelope shape on **stderr** with `status: "error"`:
109	
110	```
111	SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"success|error","brief_path":"…|null","skill_name":"…","version":"…|null","language":"…|null","scope_type":"…|null","exit_code":0,"halt_reason":null}
112	```
113	
114	`status` is `"success"` on the terminal happy path, `"error"` on any HALT. `halt_reason` is one of: `null` (success), `"input-missing"`, `"input-invalid"`, `"forge-tier-missing"`, `"target-inaccessible"`, `"gh-auth-failed"`, `"write-failed"`, `"overwrite-cancelled"`, `"user-cancelled"`. `exit_code` matches the table above.
115	
```

```
1	---
2	name: skf-quick-skill
3	description: Fast skill from a package name or GitHub URL — no brief needed. Use when the user requests a "quick skill" or "skill from URL" or "skill from package."
4	---
5	
6	# Quick Skill
7	
8	## Overview
9	
10	The fastest path to a skill — accept a GitHub URL or package name, resolve to source, extract the public API surface, and produce a best-effort SKILL.md with context snippet and metadata. No brief needed. Quick Skill is tier-unaware by design — all output is produced at community-tier quality regardless of available tools.
11	
12	## Conventions
13	
14	- Bare paths (e.g. `references/<name>.md`) resolve from the skill root.
15	- `references/` holds prompt content carved out of SKILL.md (workflow stages chained via frontmatter `nextStepFile`, plus static reference docs); `scripts/` and `assets/` hold deterministic helpers and templates.
16	- `{skill-root}` resolves to this skill's installed directory (where `customize.toml` lives, if present).
17	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
18	- `{skill-name}` resolves to the skill directory's basename.
19	
20	## Role
21	
22	You are a rapid skill compiler collaborating with a developer. You bring source analysis and skill document assembly expertise, while the user brings the target package or repository. Work together efficiently — speed is the priority.
23	
24	## Workflow Rules
25	
26	These rules apply to every step in this workflow:
27	
28	- Never fabricate content — all data must come from source extraction or user input
29	- Read each step file completely before taking any action
30	- Follow the mandatory sequence in each step exactly — do not skip, reorder, or optimize
31	- Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps
32	- Always communicate in `{communication_language}`
33	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
34	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, emit a single-line JSON progress event to **stderr** at each step's entry and exit so pipeline schedulers can stream live progress instead of post-mortem-parsing the result contract:
35	  - entry: `{"step":N,"name":"<slug>","status":"start"}`
36	  - exit (just before chaining to nextStepFile): `{"step":N,"name":"<slug>","status":"done"}`
37	  - on HARD HALT: `{"step":N,"name":"<slug>","status":"halt","exit":<code>}` instead of "done"
38	
39	  `N` is the step number (1–7) and `<slug>` is the kebab portion of the filename after the number — `resolve-target`, `ecosystem-check`, `quick-extract`, `compile`, `write-and-validate`, `finalize`, `health-check`. One line per event; do not pretty-print.
40	
41	## Stages
42	
43	| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
44	|---|------|------|--------------|
45	| 1 | Resolve Target | references/resolve-target.md | Yes |
46	| 2 | Ecosystem Check | references/ecosystem-check.md | Yes |
47	| 3 | Quick Extract | references/quick-extract.md | Yes |
48	| 4 | Compile | references/compile.md | No (review) |
49	| 5 | Write & Validate | references/write-and-validate.md | Yes |
50	| 6 | Finalize | references/finalize.md | Yes |
51	| 7 | Workflow Health Check | references/health-check.md | Yes |
52	
53	## Invocation Contract
54	
55	| Aspect | Detail |
56	|--------|--------|
57	| **Inputs** | target (GitHub URL or package name) [required for single-target mode], language_hint [optional], scope_hint [optional] |
58	| **Overrides** | `--description`, `--exports`, `--skip-snippet`, `--no-active-pointer`, `--batch <file>`, `--fail-fast` — see On Activation step 3 |
59	| **Gates** | step 1: Input Gate [use args]; step 2: Choice Gate [P] (if match); step 4: Review Gate [C/E/S/Q] |
60	| **Outputs** | SKILL.md, context-snippet.md, metadata.json, active pointer, result contract (timestamped + `-latest` copy). Snippet and active pointer can be skipped per overrides. |
61	| **Headless** | All gates auto-resolve with default action when `{headless_mode}` is true |
62	| **Exit codes** | See "Exit Codes" below |
63	
64	## Exit Codes
65	
66	Every HARD HALT in this workflow exits with a stable, documented code so headless automators can branch on the failure class without grepping message text:
67	
68	| Code | Meaning                | Raised by                                                   |
69	| ---- | ---------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
70	| 0    | success                | step 7 (terminal)                                          |
71	| 3    | resolution-failure     | step 1 §2c (prose input), step 1 §3 (registry chain failed) |
72	| 4    | write-failure          | step 5 §2 (deliverable write failed)                       |
73	| 5    | overwrite-cancelled    | step 5 §1 (user selected [N])                              |
74	| 6    | compile-cancelled      | step 4 §6 (user selected [Q])                              |
75	| 7    | finalize-blocked       | step 6 §1 (active-pointer flip refused — non-link in place) |
76	
77	Reserved: `validator-missing` may be promoted from advisory log to fatal exit code in a future revision; consumers should not assume code 8+ is unused.
78	
79	## Result Contract on HARD HALT
80	
81	In addition to the success-variant result contract written by step 6 §3, every HARD HALT must surface an **error variant** so headless automators don't silently break when `quick-skill-result-latest.json` is missing on failed runs.
82	
83	**Always (every HARD HALT, regardless of phase)** — emit a single line on **stderr**:
84	
85	```
86	SKF_QUICK_SKILL_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"error","exit_code":<N>,"phase":"<slug>","error":{"code":"<class>","message":"<short>"},"outputs":{},"summary":{},"skill_package":"<path-or-null>"}
87	```
88	
89	One line, no pretty-print. Matches the prefix-and-envelope convention used by `skf-emit-result-envelope.py`.
90	
91	**Additionally, when `{skill_package}` is known** (HALT at step 5 §1 onward) — write the same JSON object (without the `SKF_QUICK_SKILL_RESULT_JSON: ` prefix) to disk:
92	
93	```
94	{skill_package}/quick-skill-result-{YYYYMMDD-HHmmss}.json
95	{skill_package}/quick-skill-result-latest.json   (copy, not symlink)
96	```
97	
98	so consumers that hardcode the `-latest.json` path see a deterministic file even on failed runs. HALTs at step 1/02/03/04 cannot write to disk because `{skill_package}` is computed only in step 5 §1; for those, the stderr envelope plus exit code is the contract.
99	
100	**Schema:**
101	
102	| Field           | Type           | Notes                                                                                                       |
103	| --------------- | -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
104	| `status`        | string         | always `"error"` for HARD HALTs                                                                             |
105	| `exit_code`     | integer        | matches the Exit Codes table                                                                                |
106	| `phase`         | string         | step slug where the HALT occurred (e.g. `resolve-target`, `compile`)                                        |
107	| `error.code`    | string         | one of: `resolution-failure`, `write-failure`, `overwrite-cancelled`, `compile-cancelled`, `finalize-blocked` |
108	| `error.message` | string         | the user-facing message that was displayed                                                                  |
109	| `error.details` | any            | optional — phase-specific context (e.g. the failed file path)                                               |
110	| `outputs`       | object         | empty `{}` on early HALTs; partial when files were already written                                          |
111	| `summary`       | object         | empty `{}` on early HALTs                                                                                   |
112	| `skill_package` | string \| null | absolute path when known, `null` when HALT preceded step 5 §1                                              |
113	
114	## On Activation
115	
116	1. Read `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/config.yaml` and `{forger_root}/preferences.yaml` in parallel (one batched tool-call message — they are independent files), then resolve:
117	   - From config: `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `document_output_language`, `skills_output_folder`, `forge_data_folder`
118	   - From preferences: `headless_mode` (default false)
119	
120	2. **Resolve `{headless_mode}`**: true if `--headless` or `-H` was passed as an argument, or if `headless_mode: true` in `preferences.yaml`. Default: false.
121	
122	3. **Parse CLI overrides** — capture optional override flags into the workflow context as `{overrides}`. Each override is opt-in; when omitted, the workflow runs as today.
123	
124	   | Flag | Effect |
125	   | --- | --- |
126	   | `--description "<string>"` | Override the LLM-derived description in step 4 §2 (used in SKILL.md frontmatter and metadata.json). Subject to the same agentskills.io length (1–1024 chars) and voice (third-person) checks as extracted descriptions. |
127	   | `--exports "<name1,name2,...>"` | Override the extracted export list. Parse as comma-separated; trim whitespace per item; skip empty items. Used in step 4 §2 Key Exports and the count-derived metadata stats. |
128	   | `--skip-snippet` | Skip context-snippet.md generation in step 4 §3 and its write in step 5 §2. Artifact omitted from `outputs`; step 5 §5 advisory snippet validation reports a "skipped" entry. |
129	   | `--no-active-pointer` | Skip the active-pointer flip in step 6 §1. Deliverables still land in `{skill_package}` but `{skill_group}/active` is not updated. Useful for batch automators that flip pointers in a separate stage. |
130	   | `--batch <file>` | Run the workflow against a list of targets from a text file rather than a single argument. Implies `--headless` (gates cannot be human-driven across N targets). See "Batch Mode" below for input format and summary contract. Single-target overrides above apply globally to every target in the batch. |
131	   | `--fail-fast` | Only meaningful with `--batch`. Abort the whole batch on the first per-target failure instead of recording the failure in the summary and proceeding to the next target. |
132	
133	4. **If `--batch` is set**, force `{headless_mode} = true` (log "headless: coerced by --batch" if it was false), read the batch file, and parse the target list per "Batch Mode" below. Continue at step 5; the batch loop documented in "Batch Mode" wraps the step 1 → step 7 pipeline that follows.
134	
135	5. Load, read the full file, and then execute `references/resolve-target.md` to begin the workflow. (In batch mode, control returns here for each subsequent target after step 7 completes; see "Batch Mode" below.)
136	
137	## Batch Mode
138	
139	When `--batch <file>` is supplied, quick-skill processes a list of targets from a text file in sequence rather than a single target from arguments. Designed for unattended bulk runs (CI pipelines, mass-rebuilds, the skf-batch-skills meta-workflow when it lands).
140	
141	### Input format
142	
143	One target per line. Empty lines and lines starting with `#` (after optional leading whitespace) are ignored. Each non-empty line has the same shape as the single-target `target` argument, with optional space-separated per-line modifiers:
144	
145	```
146	# A batch input file.
147	lodash
148	@vercel/og
149	cognee@0.5.0
150	https://github.com/foo/bar
151	https://github.com/foo/bar@2.1.0-beta
152	
153	# Per-line modifiers — overrides for THIS target only:
154	lodash language=javascript scope=src/
155	cognee@0.5.0 language=python scope=cognee/api/
156	```
157	
158	Recognised per-line modifiers:
159	
160	| Modifier | Effect (this target only) |
161	| --- | --- |
162	| `language=<lang>` | Sets `language_hint` for this target — same effect as the optional `language_hint` input on a single-target run. |
163	| `scope=<path>` | Sets `scope_hint` for this target — same effect as the optional `scope_hint` input on a single-target run. |
164	
165	Per-line modifiers shadow the global `--description` / `--exports` / `--skip-snippet` / `--no-active-pointer` overrides only when those override fields are not set. Global overrides apply to every target unless a future modifier extends per-line override syntax.
166	
167	### Execution
168	
169	`--batch` implies `--headless`. The batch loop runs the full quick-skill pipeline (steps 1–7) for each target in file order:
170	
171	1. Set `target`, `target_version`, `language_hint`, `scope_hint` from the batch line into the workflow context.
172	2. Execute steps 1–7 per the normal pipeline.
173	3. After step 7 completes (success or HARD HALT), record the per-target outcome (target, status, exit_code, skill_package, error.code) into the batch result list.
174	4. If `--fail-fast` is set and the target failed, exit the batch loop immediately. Otherwise continue with the next target.
175	
176	Per-target output lands in `{skill_package}/` as today, with the per-target result contract at `{skill_package}/quick-skill-result-latest.json` (success or error variant per "Result Contract on HARD HALT" above).
177	
178	### Batch summary contract
179	
180	After the last target completes (or `--fail-fast` triggers an early exit), write the batch summary at:
181	
182	```
183	{skills_output_folder}/_batch/quick-skill-batch-{YYYYMMDD-HHmmss}.json
184	{skills_output_folder}/_batch/quick-skill-batch-latest.json   (copy, not symlink)
185	```
186	
187	Schema:
188	
189	```json
190	{
191	  "skill": "skf-quick-skill",
192	  "mode": "batch",
193	  "status": "success | partial | failed",
194	  "timestamp": "<ISO 8601 UTC>",
195	  "input_file": "<path passed to --batch>",
196	  "targets_total": 0,
197	  "succeeded": 0,
198	  "failed": 0,
199	  "fail_fast_triggered": false,
200	  "results": [
201	    {
202	      "target": "<line from batch file>",
203	      "status": "success | error",
204	      "exit_code": 0,
205	      "skill_package": "<absolute path or null>",
206	      "error_code": null
207	    }
208	  ]
209	}
210	```
211	
212	`status` resolves as: `"success"` when `failed == 0`; `"partial"` when `failed > 0 && succeeded > 0`; `"failed"` when `succeeded == 0`. `fail_fast_triggered` is `true` only when `--fail-fast` aborted the loop early — `targets_total` then reflects the count actually attempted, not the file's line count.
213	
214	### Headless events
215	
216	Batch mode emits per-target boundary events on stderr in addition to the per-step events documented in Workflow Rules:
217	
218	```
219	{"batch":<n>,"target":"<target>","status":"start"}
220	{"batch":<n>,"target":"<target>","status":"done","exit":<code>}
221	{"batch":<n>,"target":"<target>","status":"fail","exit":<code>,"error_code":"<class>"}
222	```
223	
224	`<n>` is the 1-based index of the target in the parsed list. After the loop ends, emit one final batch-summary event:
225	
226	```
227	{"batch_summary":true,"targets_total":N,"succeeded":K,"failed":M,"status":"<...>","fail_fast_triggered":<bool>}
228	```
229	
230	### Exit code
231	
232	The batch process exits with code `0` when `failed == 0`, otherwise with the exit code of the first failed target (so automators that already branch on the single-target exit-code map continue to work without batch-specific handling). When `--fail-fast` triggers, the exit code is the failing target's code.
233	
```

}`\n\n**Companion file:** `src/shared/references/description-guard-protocol.md` — single source of truth for the 4-phase prose; both skills' stage files load it via frontmatter and stop restating the protocol inline.\n\n### A2–A6. update-skill deterministic ops (U1)\n\nPer quality-report A1 \"Lift Deterministic Operations into Scripts\" (HIGH):\n\n| Script | Extract from | Subcommand sketch |\n|---|---|---|\n| `skf-build-change-manifest.py` | `skf-update-skill/references/detect-changes.md` §3 | inputs: provenance map + current source tree → emits change manifest JSON |\n| `skf-provenance-gap-dispatch.py` | `references/detect-changes.md` (§1c gap-driven dispatcher) | reads drift report + brief scope → emits add/keep/drop classifications |\n| `skf-hash-content.py` | scattered SHA-256 prose | `hash <path>` → emits sha256 hex |\n| `skf-check-workspace-drift.py` | `references/remote-source-resolution.md` (git guard) | wraps `git status --porcelain` checks, emits clean/dirty enum |\n| `skf-update-active-symlink.py` | `references/write.md` (atomic symlink update) | atomic relink via temp + rename; emits before/after target |\n\n### A7–A9. create-skill deterministic ops (C2)\n\nPer quality-report C2 \"Script delegation for deterministic ops\" (HIGH):\n\n| Script | Extract from | Subcommand sketch |\n|---|---|---|\n| `skf-detect-scripts-assets.py` | `skf-create-skill/references/extract.md` §4c | walks source tree → classifies scripts vs assets per skill-sections.md rules |\n| `skf-resolve-authoritative-files.py` | `references/extract.md` §2a (authoritative-files protocol, ~95 lines) | resolves brief `scope.include` + canonical-doc heuristics → ranked file list |\n| `skf-validate-brief-schema.py` | `references/load-brief.md` §3 | validates loaded brief against `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skill-brief.v1.json` (already exists — script just wraps `jsonschema` invocation with skill-friendly error formatting) |\n\n## Workstream B — structural changes in create-skill\n\n### B1. Carve `extract.md` §2a to its own file (C3, medium)\n\n`src/skf-create-skill/references/extract.md` is 379 lines. §2a \"Authoritative Files Protocol\" embedded inside it accounts for ~95 lines. Convert to `xargs -P 4` or background+wait pattern.\n- `references/sub/fetch-docs.md` §3 — runs up to 10 subpage fetches sequentially. Same pattern.\n\n## Workstream C — headless improvements in update-skill (U2, U3)\n\n### C1. `headless_decisions[]` in result contract (U2)\n\nCurrently auto-resolutions are logged inline as evidence-report lines but not surfaced as a structured field. Add a top-level `headless_decisions[]` array to `src/shared/scripts/schemas/skf-update-result-envelope.v1.json` (file may not yet exist — model on `skf-setup-result-envelope.v1.json`); each entry is `{ gate, default_action, taken_action, reason }`.\n\n### C2. `--dry-run` and `--detect-only` flags (U2)\n\n- `--dry-run`: run detect-changes + plan, emit envelope, skip write/merge\n- `--detect-only`: run detect-changes only, emit change manifest, exit\n\nMirror the CLI-knob pattern from skf-setup (`--orphan-action`, `--ccc-skip-index`, `--quiet` — see commit `3dd864cd`).\n\n### C3. Headless mode should fail-fast if lock held; interactive should prompt.\n\n## Workstream D — brief-skill wiring (B1)\n\n`customize.toml` ships three asset-path scalars (`description_voice_examples_path`, `scope_templates_path`, `brief_schema_path`). Recommend tackling A1 (Description Guard) first since it eliminates duplication and unblocks both create-skill and update-skill wiring.\n\nTracks: PR #306, PR #305","title":"Elevate skf-core skills — PR #2: shared scripts + headless improvements + §2a carve"}

{
  "command": "gh issue create --title \"Elevate SKF Feature workflows — substantive deferrals from quality-analysis pass\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Background\n\nPR #324 shipped the **mechanical/parity** elevation work for the 5 SKF Feature workflow skills (skf-quick-skill, skf-create-stack-skill, skf-analyze-source, skf-audit-skill, skf-test-skill): customize.toml × 5, language headers across 50+ reference files, Exit Codes + Result Contract sections, `[X]` cancel-lines on interactive gates, bug fixes, defensive-rule trim. A follow-up Tightening PR will sweep the 4 remaining Broken items + low-severity polish.\n\nThis issue tracks the **deferred substantial work** identified in the same quality-analysis pass — items requiring new shared scripts, structural carves, new headless surfaces, or design conversations.\n\nEach elevation workstream maps to a peer-Excellent pattern (skf-brief-skill / skf-create-skill / skf-update-skill); references to skill files use bare-path-from-repo-root form.\n\n---\n\n## Workstream A — New shared scripts (LLM → deterministic)\n\nPattern from PRs #308–#316: each extraction is PEP 723 inline-metadata, JSON I/O contract, paired test in `test/test-skf-*.py`, wired into `test:python`, invoked from stage prompts via `uv run`.\n\n### A1. `skf-scan-manifests.py` (cross-skill — SS + AN)\n\nReplaces ~150 lines of LLM-orchestrated manifest scanning across:\n- `src/skf-create-stack-skill/references/detect-manifests.md` §2–§3 (8-ecosystem dependency parser)\n- `src/skf-analyze-source/references/scan-project.md` §2 (manifest + entry-point + service-config enumeration across `project_paths[]`)\n\nSubcommands: `scan <root> --ecosystems=auto` → emits `{manifests: [{path, ecosystem, deps: [...]}], total_unique: N, monorepo: bool}`.\n\n### A2. `skf-pair-intersect.py` (SS)\n\nExtract from `src/skf-create-stack-skill/references/detect-integrations.md` §1 (file-list intersection prune, currently \"MANDATORY first pass\" implemented in prose).\n\nSubcommands: `intersect --libraries <json>` → emits `{pairs: [{a, b, intersection_count, files: [...]}]}` sorted by intersection size.\n\n### A3. `skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py` (SS)\n\nExtract compose-mode discovery from `src/skf-create-stack-skill/references/parallel-extract.md` §0 (skill discovery + export-manifest fallback + symlink-fallback + cycle guard + metadata-hash). Currently per-skill loop in parent context — heavy implicit-read.\n\nSubcommands: `enumerate <skills-root>` → emits `{skills: [{name, exports, exports_source, confidence, metadata_hash}], cycles: [], warnings: []}`.\n\n### A4. `skf-compare-file-hashes.py` (AS)\n\nReplaces SHA-256 prefix-strip + per-file hash compute prose at `src/skf-audit-skill/references/structural-diff.md` §4b. LLM has no native SHA-256; per-file shell-out today.\n\nSubcommands: `compare <provenance-map.json> <source-root>` → emits `{added: [], removed: [], changed: [{path, stored_hash, current_hash}]}`. Prefix normalization lives in script.\n\n### A5. `skf-load-provenance.py` (AS)\n\nCentralizes provenance-map walk + canonicalization transforms scattered across `src/skf-audit-skill/references/{init,structural-diff}.md`.\n\nSubcommands: `normalize <map.json>` → emits `{bounded_scan_files: [...], is_stack_skill, legacy_stack_provenance, source_root, baseline_commit, baseline_ref, reexport_map: {...}}`.\n\n### A6. `skf-scan-skill-md-structure.py` (TS)\n\nReplaces ~3 LLM-orchestrated checks at `src/skf-test-skill/references/coherence-check.md` §2.1–§2.6: required-section presence (synonym-aware), markdown-fence balance, language-tag scan, table column drift, exports-in-Usage cross-grep.\n\nSubcommands:\n- `scan <skill-md>` → emits `{unbalanced_fences, bare_opening_fences[], table_drift[]}`\n- `scan <skill-md> --required-sections` → emits `{description: {satisfied, matched_synonym}, usage: {...}, api_surface: {...}}`\n\n### A7. `skf-disqualify-candidates.py` (AN)\n\nExtract boundary disqualification heuristics from `src/skf-analyze-source/references/identify-units.md` (file count < 3, LoC < 100, `node_modules/` / `vendor/` / `dist/` detection).\n\nSubcommands: `filter --boundaries <json>` → emits `{kept: [...], dropped: [{name, reason}]}`.\n\n## Workstream B — compute-score.py modernization (TS)\n\n### B1. Unit tests + argparse migration\n\n`src/skf-test-skill/scripts/compute-score.py` centralizes floor logic that drives correctness (false-PASS catastrophic per scoring-rules.md). Currently:\n- No `scripts/tests/` directory\n- Raw `sys.argv[1]` parsing, no `--help`\n- M3 fixture matrix at `references/scoring-rules.md:113–141` declares fixtures (`test/fixtures/compute-score-contract.json`) that don't exist\n\n**Fix:**\n- Create `test/test-compute-score-contract.py` seeded from the M3 matrix's 4 equivalence classes (A/B/C/D) + INCONCLUSIVE floor cases + threshold cap\n- Wire into `package.json` `test:python`\n- Replace `sys.argv` block with argparse: `--json-input` (positional or stdin), `--help` showing the input schema\n\n## Workstream C — Determinism: subagent fan-out + return-format discipline\n\n### C1. AN `map-and-detect.md` per-unit subagent fan-out\n\n`src/skf-analyze-source/references/map-and-detect.md` §2 references \"subprocess Pattern 2 when available\" without defining what that means; per-unit analysis is sequential by default. For projects with 10+ qualifying units, this dominates wall-clock.\n\n**Fix:** make the per-unit analysis an explicit parallel fan-out: spawn one subagent per qualifying unit, each returns ONLY `{unit_name, files_count, exports_count, export_pattern, api_surface, scripts_assets, ccc_signals, strategy_used, confidence}`. Parent does not read unit source files before delegating (implicit-read trap).\n\nAlso resolve the orphaned \"Pattern N\" references — either define them inline or drop the labels.\n\n### C2. TS subagent return-format consistency\n\n`src/skf-test-skill/references/coverage-check.md` §1 has the \"ONLY return X\" discipline. §1b cross-check and contextual-coherence §3–§4 delegate to subagents without re-asserting it.\n\n**Fix:** audit every subagent invocation in `coverage-check.md` and `coherence-check.md`; add `Return ONLY <schema>. No prose, no commentary, no markdown.` to each.\n\n## Workstream D — Headless surface extensions\n\n### D1. New CLI flags (TS)\n\n`src/skf-test-skill/references/report.md` §4b (Discovery Testing) and §7 (Workflow Health Check dispatch) are mandatory. For a CI matrix running test-skill across 50 skills, both dominate wall-clock.\n\n- `--no-discovery` → skip §4b.1–§4b.3 (Discovery Testing block), log Info note\n- `--no-health-check` → skip §7 dispatch, exit at §6b\n- `--tier=<Quick|Forge|Forge+|Deep>` → bypass `forge-tier.yaml` sidecar requirement (for fresh CI shells where setup hasn't run)\n\n### D2. New CLI flags (AN)\n\n`src/skf-analyze-source/SKILL.md` Invocation Contract currently has no headless arg surface beyond `--headless`.\n\n- `--project-path <path>`\n- `--scope-hint <text>`\n- `--intent-hint <text>` (also drives the recommendation ranking)\n\n### D3. Workflow scalars\n\n- `default_threshold = 80` in TS customize.toml — wires to score.md §1 reads `{workflow.default_threshold}` and passes into compute-score JSON; CLI override still wins\n- `on_complete = \"\"` hook scalars in TS, AN, AS customize.toml — invoked with `--result-path=...` after atomic write; downstream pipelines (slack, dashboard, CI) snap on without forking\n\n## Workstream E — Structural carves\n\n### E1. Carve Batch Mode out of skf-quick-skill SKILL.md\n\n`src/skf-quick-skill/SKILL.md` includes ~95 lines of Batch Mode + boundary-event JSON sub-schema at SKILL.md scope. Single-target users (the common case) pay weight for this on every activation.\n\n**Fix:** carve to `references/batch-mode.md` with input format, execution loop, summary contract, headless events, exit code. SKILL.md retains a 3–4-line summary + path pointer. Survives compaction because On Activation step 4 explicitly loads when `--batch` is set.\n\n### E2. Carve SS provenance-map schemas to assets/\n\n`src/skf-create-stack-skill/references/generate-output.md` is 328 lines / 3428 tokens. ~80 lines of inline JSON schema templates (provenance-map.json code-mode + compose-mode) could lift to `assets/provenance-map-schema.md` with `{provenanceMapSchema}` frontmatter routing.\n\n## Workstream F — Decision-Log Workspace protocols\n\n### F1. DLW resume protocol for skf-create-stack-skill\n\n10-step workflow with parallel extraction, atomic-write commit, symlink flip. If context compacts between step 4 and step 6 the user loses state. Currently no `.decision-log.md` workspace; re-running rebuilds from scratch.\n\n**Minimum:** add a \"if `{forge_version}/in-progress.json` exists, offer to resume from the last completed step\" check at the top of `init.md`.\n\n**Full:** write a `.decision-log.md` to `{forge_version}/` as the load-bearing artifact (peer skills do this for the same reason).\n\n### F2. DLW resume / re-audit hint for skf-audit-skill\n\nDrift reports are timestamped — workflow always creates a new one. There's no detection of an existing recent report for the same skill. A user who re-runs after fixing one finding gets a fresh report with no diff against the prior.\n\n**Fix:** at `init.md` §6, before creating the new report, glob `{forge_version}/drift-report-*.md` and if a recent (< 7 days) report exists for the same `audit_ref`, surface a soft gate: `[F] Fresh audit (default) / [D] Diff against prior report / [R] Resume the prior report`. Headless default = F.\n\n## Workstream G — Polish (low ROI, includes only if high-confidence quick wins)\n\n- AN: `intent-before-ingestion` question in `init.md` (low-effort UX win)\n- AN: bulk-confirm shortcut in `recommend.md` (`all-Y` / `all-N` then flip exceptions)\n- AS: degraded-mode confidence banner in drift-report-template.md header\n- TS: spec-ID glossary (B1/B2/.../M5/S8 inline codes currently lack an anchor)\n\n## Validation gates (same as PR #324)\n\n- `node tools/validate-skills.js`\n- `node tools/validate-file-refs.js --strict`\n- `node test/test-installation-components.js`\n- `node test/test-workflow-state.js`\n- `node test/test-cli-integration.js`\n- `node test/test-knowledge-base.js`\n- `npm run test:python` (new test files must be appended to the `test:python` script enumeration in `package.json`)\n- `npm run lint`, `lint:md`, `format:check`\n\n## Effort estimate\n\n- Workstream A: ~6 scripts × ~30 min each including tests + wiring ≈ 3–4 hours\n- Workstream B: ~45 min (compute-score test file + argparse)\n- Workstream C: ~30 min (fan-out wiring + return-format audit)\n- Workstream D: ~90 min (3 flags + 2 hook scalars + wiring)\n- Workstream E: ~60 min (Batch Mode carve + provenance schemas)\n- Workstream F: ~90 min minimum for the resume-hint flavors; full DLW ~3 hours each\n\nTracks: PR #324\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open tracking issue for substantive deferrals"
}

66	| Code | Meaning                | Raised by                                                   |

68	| 0    | success                | step 7 (terminal)                                          |
69	| 3    | resolution-failure     | step 1 §2c (prose input), step 1 §3 (registry chain failed) |

75	Reserved: `validator-missing` may be promoted from advisory log to fatal exit code in a future revision; consumers should not assume code 8+ is unused.

77	## Result Contract on HARD HALT
78	
79	In addition to the success-variant result contract written by step 6 §3, every HARD HALT must surface an **error variant** so headless automators don't silently break when `quick-skill-result-latest.json` is missing on failed runs.
80	
81	**Always (every HARD HALT, regardless of phase)** — emit a single line on **stderr**:
82	
83	```
84	SKF_QUICK_SKILL_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"error","exit_code":<N>,"phase":"<slug>","error":{"code":"<class>","message":"<short>"},"outputs":{},"summary":{},"skill_package":"<path-or-null>"}
85	```
86	
87	One line, no pretty-print. Matches the prefix-and-envelope convention used by `skf-emit-result-envelope.py`.

46	2. Counts and categorizes exports based on forge tier:
47	   - **Quick tier:** Count files by type, identify index/barrel files, list directory structure
48	   - **Forge tier:** Parse export statements, identify public API surface, count exported functions/classes/types
49	   - **Forge+ tier:** All Forge analysis plus:
50	     - If `tools.ccc` is true: run `ccc_bridge.search("{unit_name} exports public API", top_k=15)` — **Tool resolution:** `/ccc` skill search (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `ccc search` CLI; see `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` — to discover semantically relevant files beyond directory scan
51	     - Merge CCC-discovered files with scoped file list — files from CCC that are within the unit's directory are added to the analysis queue

53	   - **Deep tier:** All Forge analysis plus:

- **Universal cancel-line affordance** — at any interactive prompt the user may type `cancel`, `exit`, `:q`, or select the `[X] Cancel and exit` menu option (where surfaced) to leave cleanly. HARD HALT with **exit code 6 (user-cancelled)** and emit the error result contract per "Result Contract on HARD HALT" with `error.code: "user-cancelled"`. In step 4 §6 the equivalent affordance is `[Q] Quit without writing` — same exit code, same envelope contract.
- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
- If `{headless_mode}` is true, emit a single-line JSON progress event to **stderr** at each step's entry and exit so pipeline schedulers can stream live progress instead of post-mortem-parsing the result contract:
  - entry: `{"step":N,"name":"<slug>","status":"start"}`
  - exit (just before chaining to nextStepFile): `{"step":N,"name":"<slug>","status":"done"}`
  - on HARD HALT: `{"step":N,"name":"<slug>","status":"halt","exit":<code>}` instead of "done"

  `N` is the step number (1–7) and `<slug>` is the kebab portion of the filename after the number — `resolve-target`, `ecosystem-check`, `quick-extract`, `compile`, `write-and-validate`, `finalize`, `health-check`. One line per event; do not pretty-print.

## Stages

| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
|---|------|------|--------------|

- Follow the mandatory sequence in each step exactly — do not skip, reorder, or optimize
- Only load one step file at a time — never preload future steps
- If any instruction references a subprocess or tool you lack, achieve the outcome in your main context thread
- Always communicate in `{communication_language}`
- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with their default action and log each auto-decision
- **Workflow state contract — `workflow_warnings[]` accumulator (M4):** every step that emits a warning ("log a warning", "record in workflow state for the evidence report", "Warning: ...", etc.) MUST append a structured entry to a single in-memory list named `workflow_warnings[]`. Each entry has the shape `{step: "step-NN", severity: "info|warn|error", code: "<short-slug>", message: "<human text>", context: {<optional fields>}}`. Step-07 surfaces these in `evidence-report.md`; step 8 may add validation findings; step 9 §5 reads the accumulated list and renders the user-facing "Warnings" section. Do not invent a per-step warning channel — there is exactly one accumulator for the whole workflow.

## Stages

| # | Step | File | Auto-proceed |
|---|------|------|--------------|
| 1 | Initialize & Mode Detection | references/init.md | No (confirm) |
| 2 | Detect Manifests | references/detect-manifests.md | Yes |
| 3 | Rank & Confirm Libraries | references/rank-and-confirm.md | No (confirm) |
| 4 | Parallel Extract | references/parallel-extract.md | Yes |
| 5 | Detect Integrations | references/detect-integrations.md | Yes |
| 6 | Compile Stack | references/compile-stack.md | No (review) |
| 7 | Generate Output | references/generate-output.md | Yes |
| 8 | Validate | references/validate.md | Yes |
| 9 | Report | references/report.md | Yes |
| 10 | Workflow Health Check | references/health-check.md | Yes |

The genius compression was the parallel-scan + cross-skill synthesis upfront: 5 simultaneous quality-analysis agents → one ranked findings table grouped by type → one Foundation PR covering all 5 skills mechanically → one Tightening PR sweeping the long tail. > AGENT

🎉 5 Feature workflows now officially Excellent. The full elevation shipped in 2 PRs (#324 + #326) with 1 tracking issue (#325) for the substantive follow-up workstreams — a 17 → 2 PR compression vs. Core. Ready for the next batch when you are.

> DEVELOPER

Can we address the issue #325? We should end up with a minimal commits and PRs. What do you think?

> AGENT

Yes, let's. Same 2-PR genius compression pattern works here.

## Recommendation: split #325's 6 workstreams across 2 thematic PRs

**PR 1 — "Deterministic scripts + compute-score modernization"** (the mechanical half)
- Workstream A: all 7 new shared scripts (`scan-manifests`, `pair-intersect`, `enumerate-stack-skills`, `compare-file-hashes`, `load-provenance`, `scan-skill-md-structure`, `disqualify-candidates`) — each with PEP 723 + paired test + stage prompt wiring + `test:python` entry
- Workstream B: TS `compute-score.py` tests + argparse migration
- Workstream C2: TS subagent return-format consistency audit (just `Return ONLY <schema>` wording — fits the determinism theme)
- Parallel execution: dispatch 7+1 agents (one per script + one for compute-score), like Foundation
- Estimated size: ~25-30 files, +2000 -800 (heavy add, modest delete from stage prompts)

**PR 2 — "Surface extensions, structural carves, resume protocols + polish"** (the design-heavier half)
- Workstream C1: AN per-unit subagent fan-out (defines Pattern N properly)
- Workstream D: new headless flags (`--no-discovery`, `--no-health-check`, `--tier=`, `--project-path`, `--scope-hint`, `--intent-hint`) + workflow scalars (`default_threshold`, `on_complete`)
- Workstream E: Batch Mode carve in QS, provenance-map schema carve in SS
- Workstream F: DLW resume protocols for SS + AS (resume-hint flavor, not full DLW)
- Workstream G: 4 polish items (intent-before-ingestion, bulk-confirm, degraded-mode banner, spec-ID glossary or drop)
- Estimated size: ~30-40 files, +800 -400

## Tradeoff

PR 1 is large but mechanical and parallelizable; reviewers can scan script-by-script. PR 2 is design-y but each workstream is independent and contained per skill. The risk in batching this much: a single broken script in PR 1 blocks all 7, and PR 2's DLW changes are the kind of thing that benefits from a focused review.

If you want even fewer PRs (1 mega-PR), I can do it, but I'd recommend against — the script work and the surface work have different review rhythms.

1	# /// script
2	# requires-python = ">=3.10"
3	# dependencies = []
4	# ///
5	"""SKF Hash Content — SHA-256 hashing helpers for skill workflows.
6	
7	Two patterns recur across the update-skill workflow's stage prose:
8	
9	  1. **Single-file hash** — detect-changes.md §1b, when a candidate file is
10	     promoted into `brief.scope.include`, the LLM is asked to "compute SHA-256
11	     content hash of candidate.path" and emit a record
12	     `{path, heuristic, size_bytes, line_count, content_hash}` for
13	     `promoted_docs_new[]`. Deterministic file-read + hashlib.sha256.
14	
15	  2. **Bulk-compare against provenance** — detect-changes.md §Category D,
16	     where the LLM is asked to "for each file_entry: compute current SHA-256
17	     content hash, compare against stored hash" and classify entries as
18	     MODIFIED_FILE / DELETED_FILE / (UNCHANGED). Same hashing primitive,
19	     iterated, plus a stat-and-compare. NEW_FILE detection is intentionally
20	     out of scope here — that lives in `skf-detect-scripts-assets.py`.
21	
22	Subcommands:
23	  hash <path> [--include-path]
24	      Emit JSON {"content_hash": "sha256:...", "size_bytes": N, "line_count": L}
25	      for a single file. With --include-path, the record also includes
26	      "path": <as given>, so batch callers can collect records by streaming
27	      multiple invocations.
28	
29	  compare <source-root> --provenance-map <path>
30	      Read `file_entries[]` from a provenance-map JSON and classify each row
31	      against the current source tree. Emits
32	        {
33	          "comparisons": [
34	            {"source_file": "...", "classification": "UNCHANGED|MODIFIED_FILE|DELETED_FILE",
35	             "stored_hash": "sha256:...", "current_hash": "sha256:..."|null,
36	             "current_size_bytes": N|null}, ...
37	          ],
38	          "stats": {"total": N, "unchanged": U, "modified": M, "deleted": D}
39	        }
40	      The provenance file must contain `file_entries` as a top-level array
41	      OR be the array itself (handles both schemas).
42	
43	Hash format: `sha256:` prefix on a hex digest. Matches the convention used
44	by skf-detect-scripts-assets.py and the existing prose ("SHA-256 content
45	hash" — agnostic about prefix, but the prefix is the SKF convention to make
46	hashes self-describing for future algorithm migrations).
47	
48	CLI examples:
49	  uv run skf-hash-content.py hash docs/AGENTS.md
50	  uv run skf-hash-content.py hash docs/AGENTS.md --include-path
51	  uv run skf-hash-content.py compare /path/to/source \\
52	      --provenance-map /path/to/provenance-map.json
53	
54	Exit codes:
55	  0  — operation succeeded (including: file in compare missing on disk;
56	       its classification is DELETED_FILE, not an error)
57	  1  — user error (bad path, malformed JSON, missing required field)
58	"""
59	
60	from __future__ import annotations
61	
62	import argparse
63	import hashlib
64	import json
65	import sys
66	from pathlib import Path
67	
68	
69	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
70	# Hashing primitives
71	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
72	
73	
74	def sha256_of_file(path: Path) -> str:
75	    """SHA-256 of file content, with sha256: prefix."""
76	    h = hashlib.sha256()
77	    with path.open("rb") as fh:
78	        for chunk in iter(lambda: fh.read(65536), b""):
79	            h.update(chunk)
80	    return "sha256:" + h.hexdigest()
81	
82	
83	def count_lines(path: Path) -> int:
84	    """Count newlines in file content; 0 if unreadable."""
85	    n = 0
86	    try:
87	        with path.open("rb") as fh:
88	            for chunk in iter(lambda: fh.read(65536), b""):
89	                n += chunk.count(b"\n")
90	    except OSError:
91	        return 0
92	    return n
93	
94	
95	def hash_record(path: Path, *, include_path: bool = False) -> dict:
96	    """Build a single-file record: content_hash + size_bytes + line_count."""
97	    rec: dict = {
98	        "content_hash": sha256_of_file(path),
99	        "size_bytes": path.stat().st_size,
100	        "line_count": count_lines(path),
101	    }
102	    if include_path:
103	        rec = {"path": path.as_posix(), **rec}
104	    return rec
105	
106	
107	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
108	# Provenance comparison
109	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
110	
111	
112	UNCHANGED = "UNCHANGED"
113	MODIFIED_FILE = "MODIFIED_FILE"
114	DELETED_FILE = "DELETED_FILE"
115	
116	
117	def load_file_entries(provenance_path: Path) -> list[dict]:
118	    """Extract the file_entries list from a provenance file.
119	
120	    Accepts two shapes:
121	      - top-level object with a `file_entries` key (canonical)
122	      - top-level array of entries (already-extracted)
123	
124	    Returns a copy of the list. Raises ValueError on malformed JSON or
125	    missing key.
126	    """
127	    try:
128	        data = json.loads(provenance_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
129	    except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError) as exc:
130	        raise ValueError(f"failed to read provenance file {provenance_path}: {exc}") from exc
131	
132	    if isinstance(data, list):
133	        return list(data)
134	    if isinstance(data, dict):
135	        entries = data.get("file_entries")
136	        if entries is None:
137	            raise ValueError(
138	                f"provenance file {provenance_path} has no `file_entries` field"
139	            )
140	        if not isinstance(entries, list):
141	            raise ValueError(
142	                f"`file_entries` in {provenance_path} is not an array"
143	            )
144	        return list(entries)
145	    raise ValueError(
146	        f"provenance file {provenance_path} must be an object or array; "
147	        f"got {type(data).__name__}"
148	    )
149	
150	
151	def classify_entry(source_root: Path, entry: dict) -> dict:
152	    """Classify a single file_entry against the current source tree."""
153	    source_file = entry.get("source_file")
154	    stored_hash = entry.get("content_hash")
155	    if not isinstance(source_file, str):
156	        raise ValueError(f"file_entry missing required `source_file`: {entry!r}")
157	
158	    current = (source_root / source_file).resolve()
159	    # guard against ../.. escapes — resolved path must stay inside source_root
160	    try:
161	        current.relative_to(source_root.resolve())
162	    except ValueError:
163	        return {
164	            "source_file": source_file,
165	            "classification": DELETED_FILE,
166	            "stored_hash": stored_hash,
167	            "current_hash": None,
168	            "current_size_bytes": None,
169	        }
170	
171	    if not current.is_file():
172	        return {
173	            "source_file": source_file,
174	            "classification": DELETED_FILE,
175	            "stored_hash": stored_hash,
176	            "current_hash": None,
177	            "current_size_bytes": None,
178	        }
179	
180	    current_hash = sha256_of_file(current)
181	    if current_hash == stored_hash:
182	        classification = UNCHANGED
183	    else:
184	        classification = MODIFIED_FILE
185	    return {
186	        "source_file": source_file,
187	        "classification": classification,
188	        "stored_hash": stored_hash,
189	        "current_hash": current_hash,
190	        "current_size_bytes": current.stat().st_size,
191	    }
192	
193	
194	def compare(source_root: Path, provenance_path: Path) -> dict:
195	    entries = load_file_entries(provenance_path)
196	    comparisons = [classify_entry(source_root, entry) for entry in entries]
197	    counts = {UNCHANGED: 0, MODIFIED_FILE: 0, DELETED_FILE: 0}
198	    for c in comparisons:
199	        counts[c["classification"]] += 1
200	    return {
201	        "comparisons": comparisons,
202	        "stats": {
203	            "total": len(comparisons),
204	            "unchanged": counts[UNCHANGED],
205	            "modified": counts[MODIFIED_FILE],
206	            "deleted": counts[DELETED_FILE],
207	        },
208	    }
209	
210	
211	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
212	# CLI
213	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
214	
215	
216	def _cmd_hash(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
217	    path = Path(args.path)
218	    if not path.is_file():
219	        print(f"error: file not found: {path}", file=sys.stderr)
220	        return 1
221	    rec = hash_record(path, include_path=args.include_path)
222	    json.dump(rec, sys.stdout)
223	    sys.stdout.write("\n")
224	    return 0
225	
226	
227	def _cmd_compare(args: argparse.Namespace) -> int:
228	    source_root = Path(args.source_root)
229	    provenance = Path(args.provenance_map)
230	    if not source_root.is_dir():
231	        print(f"error: source root not a directory: {source_root}", file=sys.stderr)
232	        return 1
233	    if not provenance.is_file():
234	        print(f"error: provenance map not found: {provenance}", file=sys.stderr)
235	        return 1
236	    try:
237	        result = compare(source_root, provenance)
238	    except ValueError as exc:
239	        print(f"error: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
240	        return 1
241	    json.dump(result, sys.stdout, indent=2)
242	    sys.stdout.write("\n")
243	    return 0
244	
245	
246	def _build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
247	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
248	        prog="skf-hash-content",
249	        description="SHA-256 hashing helpers: single-file record or bulk-compare against provenance.",
250	    )
251	    sub = parser.add_subparsers(dest="cmd", required=True)
252	
253	    p_hash = sub.add_parser("hash", help="emit hash record for a single file")
254	    p_hash.add_argument("path", help="path to the file")
255	    p_hash.add_argument(
256	        "--include-path",
257	        action="store_true",
258	        help="include the path string in the emitted record",
259	    )
260	    p_hash.set_defaults(func=_cmd_hash)
261	
262	    p_cmp = sub.add_parser(
263	        "compare",
264	        help="classify provenance file_entries[] against current source tree",
265	    )
266	    p_cmp.add_argument("source_root", help="path to the source tree root")
267	    p_cmp.add_argument(
268	        "--provenance-map",
269	        required=True,
270	        help="path to provenance-map.json (object with file_entries[] or bare array)",
271	    )
272	    p_cmp.set_defaults(func=_cmd_compare)
273	
274	    return parser
275	
276	
277	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
278	    parser = _build_parser()
279	    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
280	    return args.func(args)
281	
282	
283	if __name__ == "__main__":
284	    raise SystemExit(main())
285	
```

2	"""Tests for skf-hash-content.py.
3	
4	Covers:
5	  - hash: content_hash + size_bytes + line_count; --include-path flag
6	  - compare: UNCHANGED / MODIFIED_FILE / DELETED_FILE classification

10	"""
11

13

15	import importlib.util
16	import json
17	import subprocess
18	import sys
19	from pathlib import Path
20	
21

35	
36	def _expected_hash(content: bytes) -> str:
37	    return "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(content).hexdigest()
38	
39	
40	def _write(path: Path, content: str) -> Path:
41	    # binary write so newline-translation on Windows doesn't change
42	    # size_bytes / SHA-256 vs the bytes the test passed in
43	    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
44	    path.write_bytes(content.encode("utf-8"))
45	    return path
46	
47	
48	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
49	# hash subcommand
50	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------
51	
52	
53	class TestHash:
54	    def test_basic_record(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:
55	        content = "hello world\nsecond line\n"
56	        path = _write(tmp_path / "f.txt", content)
57	        rec = mod.hash_record(path)
58	        assert rec == {
59	            "content_hash": _expected_hash(content.encode()),
60	            "size_bytes": len(content),
61	            "line_count": 2,
62	        }
63	
64	    def test_include_path(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:
65	        path = _write(tmp_path / "x.txt", "x\n")
66	        rec = mod.hash_record(path, include_path=True)
67	        # path is emitted in forward-slash form for cross-platform JSON
68	        assert rec["path"] == path.as_posix()
69	        assert "content_hash" in rec
70	
71	    def test_empty_file(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:
72	        path = _write(tmp_path / "empty.txt", "")
73	        rec = mod.hash_record(path)
74	        assert rec["content_hash"] == _expected_hash(b"")
75	        assert rec["size_bytes"] == 0
76	        assert rec["line_count"] == 0
77	
78	    def test_binary_content(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:
79	        # bytes that aren't valid UTF-8 — hash should still work
80	        path = tmp_path / "blob.bin"

src/skf-test-skill/references/coverage-check.md:36:<!-- Subagent delegation: read SKILL.md + references/*.md, return compact JSON inventory -->
src/skf-test-skill/references/coverage-check.md:38:Delegate reading of the skill under test to a subagent. The subagent receives the path to SKILL.md (and the `references/` directory path if it exists) and MUST:
src/skf-test-skill/references/coverage-check.md:70:test-skill is a quality gate — it MUST NOT trust subagent output blindly. Before any downstream step consumes the inventory, the parent performs two checks and HALTs on any failure:
src/skf-test-skill/references/coverage-check.md:74:1. Strip wrapping markdown fences before parsing. Subagents frequently return JSON wrapped in a code fence — a line of three backticks (optionally followed by a language tag like `json`) preceding the JSON and a closing line of three backticks after it — despite prompt instructions to return raw JSON. When the first non-empty line of the response is three backticks (optionally with a language tag) and the last non-empty line is three backticks, remove those two fence lines before parsing. Then parse the remaining content as JSON. On parse failure of the inner content → HALT "coverage-check: subagent response not valid JSON".

122	### 2. Analyze Source Code (Tier-Dependent)
123	
124	Start from the package entry point (see 0b) and identify the public API surface. Then analyze those exports at the appropriate tier depth.
125	
126	**Quick Tier (no tools):**
127	- Read the entry point file(s) directly
128	- Identify public exports by scanning for `export` keywords, `module.exports`, `__init__.py` imports, or language-specific export patterns
129	- Compare against documented inventory by name matching
130	- Cannot verify signatures — note as "unverified" in report
131	
132	**Forge Tier (ast-grep available):**
133	DO NOT BE LAZY — For EACH source file that defines public API exports, launch a subprocess that:
134	1. Uses ast-grep to extract all exported symbols with their full signatures
135	2. Matches each export against the documented inventory
136	3. Returns structured findings:
137	
138	```json
139	{
140	  "file": "src/utils.ts",
141	  "exports_found": ["formatDate", "parseConfig", "ConfigType"],
142	  "exports_documented": ["formatDate", "parseConfig"],
143	  "missing_docs": ["ConfigType"],
144	  "signature_mismatches": [
145	    {
146	      "name": "formatDate",
147	      "source_sig": "(date: Date, format?: string) => string",
148	      "documented_sig": "(date: Date) => string",
149	      "issue": "missing optional parameter 'format'"
150	    }
151	  ]
152	}
153	```
154	
155	If subprocess unavailable, perform ast-grep analysis in main thread per file.
156	
157	**Deep Tier (ast-grep + gh + QMD):**
158	- All Forge tier checks, plus:
159	- Use gh CLI to verify source repository matches documented version
160	- Cross-check type definitions against their source declarations
161	- Verify re-exported symbols trace to their original source
162	
163	### 2b. Zero-Exports Guard
164	
165	After the source-code analysis (§2) completes, compute `total_exports` — the count of exports discovered in the source / provenance-map / metadata.json, per the stratified-scope and State 2 rules resolved in §4.
166

89	### 3. Contextual Mode: Extract References
90	
91	Scan SKILL.md for all cross-references:
92	
93	**Reference types to extract:**
94	- File path references (`./path/to/file.ts`, `../shared/types.ts`)
95	- Skill references (`See SKILL.md for {other-skill}`, `Integrates with {package}`)
96	- Type imports (`import { Type } from './module'`)
97	- Integration pattern references (middleware chains, plugin hooks, shared state)
98	- Script/asset references (`scripts/{file}`, `assets/{file}`) in SKILL.md body
99	
100	Launch a subprocess to grep/regex SKILL.md for reference patterns and return all found references with line numbers as structured JSON (`references_found[]` with line, type, target fields). If subprocess unavailable, scan in main thread.
101	
102	### 4. Contextual Mode: Validate Each Reference
103	
104	DO NOT BE LAZY — For EACH reference found, launch a subprocess that:
105	
106	1. Checks if the target exists (file exists, skill exists, type is declared)
107	2. If target exists, validates the reference is accurate:
108	   - File path references: file exists at specified path
109	   - Type imports: type is actually exported from the referenced module
110	   - Skill references: referenced skill exists in skills output folder
111	   - Integration patterns: documented pattern matches actual implementation
112	   - Script/asset references: verify the referenced file exists in the skill's `scripts/` or `assets/` directory
113	3. Returns structured validation JSON per reference (reference, line, target_exists, type_match, signature_match, issues[])
114	
115	If subprocess unavailable, validate each reference in main thread.
116	
117	4. **Scripts/assets directory check:** If a `scripts/` or `assets/` directory exists alongside SKILL.md, verify that a "Scripts & Assets" section (Section 7b) is present in SKILL.md. This directory-level check applies in both modes (naive mode performs it in Section 2; contextual mode performs it here alongside per-reference validation). Flag absence as Medium severity gap per `{scoringRulesFile}`.
118	
119	5. **Path containment:** for every resolved reference target, compute its canonical path (`os.path.realpath`) and require that it lives inside `{skillDir}` OR inside `{source_path}` (the extraction tree recorded in metadata.json). References whose canonical path escapes both roots (e.g. `../../../etc/passwd`, absolute paths to unrelated dirs, symlink redirections outside the skill or its source) are **High severity** findings: `coherence — reference escapes skill/source sandbox: {raw_ref} → {canonical_path}`. Do NOT validate the target's contents for escaping references — the escape itself is the finding.
120	
121	### 5. Contextual Mode: Check Integration Pattern Completeness
122	
123	For stack skills, verify integration patterns are complete:
```

{
  "command": "gh pr create --base v2 --head feat/skf-feature-scripts --title \"feat(skf-feature): extract 7 deterministic operations to shared scripts + modernize compute-score CLI\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nTracks issue #325 **Workstreams A + B + C2**. Replaces LLM-orchestrated prose in skf-create-stack-skill, skf-analyze-source, skf-audit-skill, and skf-test-skill with deterministic Python scripts under `src/shared/scripts/`.\n\n7 new scripts × paired pytest test files, mirroring the pattern from PRs #305 / #308–#316 (PEP 723 inline metadata, JSON I/O, `uv run` invocation from stage prompts).\n\n| # | Script | Replaces | Tests |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| A1 | `skf-scan-manifests.py` | SS `detect-manifests.md` §2–3 + AN `scan-project.md` §2 | 41 |\n| A2 | `skf-pair-intersect.py` | SS `detect-integrations.md` §1 | 33 |\n| A3 | `skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py` | SS `parallel-extract.md` §0 | 37 |\n| A4 | `skf-compare-file-hashes.py` | AS `structural-diff.md` §4b | 28 |\n| A5 | `skf-load-provenance.py` | AS `init.md` §4 + `structural-diff.md` §1 | 31 |\n| A6 | `skf-scan-skill-md-structure.py` | TS `coherence-check.md` §2.1/2.2/2.3/2.6 | 40 |\n| A7 | `skf-disqualify-candidates.py` | AN `identify-units.md` | 60 |\n| | | **Total new tests** | **270** |\n\n## Script details\n\n### A1 — `skf-scan-manifests.py`\n\n8-ecosystem dependency scanner: npm (package.json), python pip/poetry/pdm (pyproject.toml, requirements.txt, setup.py, setup.cfg, Pipfile), cargo (Cargo.toml), go (go.mod), maven (pom.xml) + gradle (build.gradle{.kts}), bundler (Gemfile), composer (composer.json), swift PM (Package.swift). Production deps only; `total_unique` excludes the `<unparsable>` sentinel; `monorepo` true iff ≥2 manifests of same ecosystem at non-overlapping depths. Subcommand: `scan <root> [--ecosystems=auto]`.\n\n### A2 — `skf-pair-intersect.py`\n\nFile-list intersection pair builder. Sorts by `intersection_count` DESC, then `(a, b)` ASC for stable output. `--top-k N` (default 20) with `truncated: true` + `total_pairs` reporting so the LLM can compose the existing S7 evidence-report warning. Subcommand: `intersect --libraries <json-or-->`.\n\n### A3 — `skf-enumerate-stack-skills.py`\n\nCompose-mode skill enumeration with four-stage exports cascade: `metadata.json` (T1) → `references/## API` (T2) → SKILL.md `## Exports` (T2) → unknown (T1-low + warning). SHA-256 hash on metadata.json (`sha256:` prefix); DFS WHITE/GRAY/BLACK cycle detection on `composes[]`; broken-symlink fallback emits warning. Subcommand: `enumerate <skills-root>`.\n\n### A4 — `skf-compare-file-hashes.py`\n\nDistinct from `skf-hash-content compare`: audit-skill needs the **inverse walk** (find NEW files in source tree not in provenance) which `skf-hash-content` doesn't do. Centralizes hash-prefix normalization so the writer-vs-reader compatibility note no longer carries as load-bearing prose. Subcommand: `compare <provenance-map.json> <source-root>` → `{added, removed, changed, stats}`.\n\n### A5 — `skf-load-provenance.py`\n\nProvenance-map normalizer: `bounded_scan_files` (union of `entries[].source_file` and `file_entries[].source_file`), `is_stack_skill` (provenance_version ≥ 2 with libraries key), `legacy_stack_provenance` (v1 + stack type), source/baseline coordinates, `reexport_map` from `__init__.py` mappings. Handles both top-level object and bare array schemas. Subcommand: `normalize <map.json>`.\n\n### A6 — `skf-scan-skill-md-structure.py`\n\nSKILL.md structural lint: fence balance, bare opening fences (no language tag), markdown-table column drift, required-section presence with synonym matching (case-insensitive, supports `Description`/`Overview`/`Purpose`/`Summary` for description; full synonym constants baked in). Subcommands: `scan <skill-md>` and `scan <skill-md> --required-sections`.\n\n### A7 — `skf-disqualify-candidates.py`\n\nBoundary candidate disqualifier: `too-few-files` (<3), `too-low-loc` (<100), `generated-code` (path contains `node_modules/`, `vendor/`, `dist/`, `build/`, `__pycache__/`, `.next/`, `target/`, `.gradle/`), `auto-generated-tag` (sentinel match in first 10 lines). LoC counting skips blank lines. Subcommand: `filter --boundaries <json-or--> --source-root <path>`.\n\n## compute-score.py modernization (Workstream B)\n\nReplaced raw `sys.argv[1]` block with argparse:\n\n```bash\n# All three forms work:\nuv run compute-score.py '<JSON>'                  # positional\nuv run compute-score.py --json-input '<JSON>'     # flag\ncat input.json | uv run compute-score.py --stdin  # piped (preferred for nested JSON)\n```\n\nModule docstring documents the full input schema. `compute_score()` function signature unchanged so existing `test/test-compute-score-contract.py` fixtures pass without modification (24 fixture suites still green). `score.md` §3b updated to prefer the `--stdin` form.\n\n## Subagent return-format discipline (Workstream C2)\n\nThree subagent invocations gained explicit \"Return ONLY ... no prose, no commentary, no markdown fences\" wording (matching §1's discipline that was already present):\n\n- `skf-test-skill/references/coverage-check.md` §2 (Forge Tier ast-grep delegation)\n- `skf-test-skill/references/coherence-check.md` §3 (reference extraction)\n- `skf-test-skill/references/coherence-check.md` §4 (per-reference validation)\n\nParent still strips wrapping markdown fences (per §1a's defensive logic). The write side (ensuring drift reports carry sufficient frontmatter to be resumable) — keep the existing frontmatter schema; if it's already capturing stepsCompleted + intermediate findings, no change needed. If it's not, leave a TODO note in the report.md frontmatter (don't expand scope here).\n\nDELIVERABLE 3 — G polish: Degraded-mode banner in drift-report template\n\n`src/skf-audit-skill/assets/drift-report-template.md` — under the existing `## Audit Summary` heading, add a new line at the top: `**Confidence Mode:** {confidence_mode}` where `{confidence_mode}` resolves to one of:\n- `normal` (default — provenance map available, full T1 findings)\n- `degraded — all findings T1-low` (when init.md detected degraded mode)\n\nThe variable resolution happens in init.md / re-index.md / report.md (depending on where mode is set). For this PR, just add the placeholder line to the template; the substitution mechanism is via the template variable that report.md §1 already uses. If the variable isn't currently set in workflow state, add a line in init.md §4 (degraded-mode confirmation gate) to set `confidence_mode = \"degraded — all findings T1-low\"` and a default `confidence_mode = \"normal\"` at init.md §0 or §1.\n\nEXECUTION RULES:\n- Use Edit tool precisely. Read each file before editing.\n- Do NOT modify any file outside src/skf-audit-skill/.\n- Do NOT touch re-index.md, structural-diff.md, semantic-diff.md, severity-classify.md, severity-rules.md, health-check.md.\n- Modify init.md (F2 + degraded mode variable), report.md (D3 hook + possibly degraded mode reference), customize.toml (D3 scalar), SKILL.md (D3 path-scalar fallback), assets/drift-report-template.md (G banner).\n\nRETURN: Files touched + line delta per file.",

7	# Batch Mode
